\displaystyle x_{t}\leq x_{t-1}-c_{\mathrm{u}}x_{t-1}^{2}\qquad\text{for all }t>0,

(68a)

then it follows that

\displaystyle x_{t}\leq\frac{1}{c_{\mathrm{u}}t+\frac{1}{x_{0}}}\qquad\text{for all }t\geq 0.

(68b)

(iii)

Suppose that
0<x_{t}<c_{x}
for all
t<t_{0}
and
x_{t_{0}}\geq c_{x}
for some quantity
c_{x}>0
. Assume that

x_{t}\geq x_{t-1}+c_{-}x_{t-1}^{2}\qquad\text{for all }0<t\leq t_{0}

(69a)

for some quantity
c_{-}>0
. Then one necessarily has

\displaystyle t_{0}\leq\frac{1+c_{-}c_{x}}{c_{-}x_{0}}.

(69b)

(iv)

Suppose that

0\leq x_{t}\leq x_{t-1}+c_{+}x_{t-1}^{2}\qquad\text{for all }0<t\leq t_{0}

(70a)

for some quantity
c_{+}>0
. Then one necessarily has

\displaystyle t_{0}\geq\frac{\frac{1}{x_{0}}-\frac{1}{x_{t_{0}}}}{c_{+}}.

(70b)

A.3
Proof of Lemma
1

(i) Let us start with state
0
. Given that this is an absorbing state and that
r(0,a_{0})=0
, we have

V^{\star}(0)=0
.

(ii) Next, we turn to the buffer states in
\mathcal{S}_{1}
and
\mathcal{S}_{2}
. For any
s_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}
, the Bellman equation gives

\displaystyle Q^{\star}(s_{1},a_{0})

\displaystyle=r(s_{1},a_{0})+\gamma V^{\star}(0)=-\gamma^{2};

(71a)

\displaystyle Q^{\star}(s_{1},a_{1})

\displaystyle=r(s_{1},a_{1})+\gamma V^{\star}(0)=\gamma^{2}.

(71b)

This in turn implies that
V^{\star}(s_{1})=Q^{\star}(s_{1},a_{1})=\gamma^{2}
.
Repeating the same argument, we arrive at
V^{\star}(s_{2})=Q^{\star}(s_{2},a_{1})=r(s_{2},a_{1})=\gamma^{4}
for any
s_{2}\in\mathcal{S}_{2}
.

(iii) We then move on to the adjoint states
\overline{1}
and
\overline{2}
. From the construction (
23
),
the Bellman equation yields

\displaystyle Q^{\star}(\overline{1},a_{0})

\displaystyle=r(\overline{1},a_{0})+\gamma V^{\star}(0)=\gamma\tau_{1}<\gamma/2,

\displaystyle Q^{\star}(\overline{1},a_{1})

\displaystyle=r(\overline{1},a_{1})+\frac{\gamma}{|\mathcal{S}_{1}|}\sum_{s_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}V^{\star}(s_{1})=\frac{\gamma}{|\mathcal{S}_{1}|}\sum_{s_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}V^{\star}(s_{1})=\gamma^{3},

where the last identity follows since
V^{\star}(s_{1})=\gamma^{2}
.
This in turn indicates that
V^{\star}(\overline{1})=\max\{Q^{\star}(\overline{1},a_{0}),Q^{\star}(\overline{1},a_{1})\}=\gamma^{3}
, provided that
\gamma^{2}\geq 1/2
.
Similarly, repeating this argument shows that
V^{\star}(\overline{2})=\gamma^{5}
, as long as
\gamma^{4}\geq 1/2
. As before, the optimal action in state
\overline{1}
(resp.
\overline{2}
) is
a_{1}
.

(iv) The next step is to determine
V^{\star}(s)
for any
s\in\{3,\cdots,H\}
. Suppose that
V^{\star}(\overline{s-2})=\gamma^{2s-3}
and
V^{\star}(\overline{s-1})=\gamma^{2s-1}
. Then the construction (
20
) together with the Bellman equation yields

\displaystyle Q^{\star}(s,a_{0})

\displaystyle=r(s,a_{0})+\gamma V^{\star}(0)=r_{s}+\gamma^{2}p\tau_{s-2}<2/3;

\displaystyle Q^{\star}(s,a_{1})

\displaystyle=r(s,a_{1})+\gamma V^{\star}(\overline{s-1})=\gamma\gamma^{2s-1}=\gamma^{2s};

\displaystyle Q^{\star}(s,a_{2})

\displaystyle=r(s,a_{2})+\gamma(1-p)V^{\star}(0)+\gamma pV^{\star}(\overline{s-2})=r_{s}+p\gamma^{2s-2}<2/3.

Consequently, one has
V^{\star}(s)=Q^{\star}(s,a_{1})=\gamma^{2s}
— namely,
a_{1}
is the optimal action — as long as
\gamma^{2s}\geq 2/3
.

(v) We then turn attention to
V^{\star}(\overline{s})
for any
\overline{s}\in\{\overline{3},\cdots,\overline{H}\}
.
Suppose that
V^{\star}(s)=\gamma^{2s}
. In view of the construction (
21
) and the Bellman equation, one has

\displaystyle Q^{\star}(\overline{s},a_{0})

\displaystyle=r(\overline{s},a_{0})+\gamma V^{\star}(0)=\gamma\tau_{s}<1/2;

\displaystyle Q^{\star}(\overline{s},a_{1})

\displaystyle=r(\overline{s},a_{1})+\gamma V^{\star}(s)=\gamma^{2s+1}.

Hence, we have
V^{\star}(\overline{s})=Q^{\star}(\overline{s},a_{1})=\gamma^{2s+1}
— with the optimal action being
a_{1}
— provided that
\gamma^{2s+1}\geq 1/2
.

(vi) Applying an induction argument based on Steps (iii), (iv) and (v), we conclude that

\displaystyle V^{\star}(s)=\gamma^{2s}\qquad\text{and}\qquad V^{\star}(\overline{s})=\gamma^{2s+1}

(72)

for all
3\leq s\leq H
, with the proviso that
\gamma^{2H}\geq 2/3
and
\gamma^{2H+1}\geq 1/2
.

(vii) In view of our MDP construction, a negative immediate reward (which is either
-\gamma^{2}
or
-\gamma^{4}
) is accrued only when the current state lies in the buffer sets
\mathcal{S}_{1}
and
\mathcal{S}_{2}
and when action
a_{0}
is executed. However, once
a_{0}
is taken, the MDP will transition to the absorbing state 0, with all subsequent rewards frozen to 0.
In conclusion, the entire MDP trajectory cannot receive negative immediate rewards more than once, thus indicating that
Q^{\pi}(s,a)\geq\min\{-\gamma^{2},-\gamma^{4}\}=-\gamma^{2}
irrespective of
\pi
and
(s,a)
.

A.4
Proof of Lemma
8

Proof of Part (i).

Before proceeding, we make note of a straightforward fact

\displaystyle V^{\pi}(0)=0,

(73)

given that state 0 is an absorbing state and
r(0,a_{0})=0
.

For any
s\in\{3,\cdots,H\}
,
the construction (
20
) together with (
73
) and the Bellman equation yields

\displaystyle Q^{\pi}(s,a_{0})

\displaystyle=r(s,a_{0})+\gamma V^{\pi}(0)=r_{s}+\gamma^{2}p\tau_{s-2};

(74a)

\displaystyle Q^{\pi}(s,a_{1})

\displaystyle=r(s,a_{1})+\gamma V^{\pi}(\overline{s-1})=\gamma V^{\pi}(\overline{s-1});

(74b)

\displaystyle Q^{\pi}(s,a_{2})

\displaystyle=r(s,a_{2})+\gamma(1-p)V^{\pi}(0)+\gamma pV^{\pi}(\overline{s-2})=r_{s}+\gamma pV^{\pi}(\overline{s-2}).

(74c)

Recalling the choices of
\tau_{s}
,
r_{s}
and
p
in (
18
),
we can continue the derivation in (
74a
) to reach

Q^{\pi}(s,a_{0})=0.5\gamma^{\frac{2s}{3}+\frac{5}{6}}+c_{\mathrm{p}}(1-\gamma)\gamma^{\frac{2s}{3}+\frac{2}{3}}

\Longrightarrow\qquad\gamma^{\frac{3}{2}}\tau_{s-1}=0.5\gamma^{\frac{2s}{3}+\frac{5}{6}}\leq Q^{\pi}(s,a_{0})\leq 0.5\gamma^{\frac{2s}{3}+\frac{1}{2}}=\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\tau_{s}.

Here, the last inequality is valid when
c_{\mathrm{p}}\leq 1/6
, given that
\gamma^{\frac{1}{3}}+\frac{1-\gamma}{6}\gamma^{\frac{1}{6}}\leq 1
holds for any
\gamma<1
.

In addition, combining (
74c
) with (
72
), we arrive at

Q^{\pi}(s,a_{2})\leq r_{s}+\gamma pV^{\star}(\overline{s-2})=0.5\gamma^{\frac{2s}{3}+\frac{5}{6}}+c_{\mathrm{p}}(1-\gamma)\gamma^{2s-2}\leq 0.5\gamma^{\frac{2s}{3}+\frac{1}{2}}=\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\tau_{s}.

This is guaranteed to hold when
c_{\mathrm{p}}\leq 1/6
, given that
\gamma^{\frac{1}{3}}+\frac{1-\gamma}{3}\gamma^{\frac{4s}{3}-\frac{5}{2}}\leq\gamma^{\frac{1}{3}}+\frac{1-\gamma}{3}\gamma^{\frac{3}{2}}\leq 1
is valid for all
\gamma<1
and
s\geq 3
.
Moreover, if one further has
V^{\pi}(\overline{s-2})\geq 0
, then it is seen from (
74c
) that

\displaystyle Q^{\pi}(s,a_{2})\geq r_{s}=0.5\gamma^{\frac{2s}{3}+\frac{5}{6}}=\gamma^{\frac{3}{2}}\tau_{s-1}.

(75)

Proof of Part (ii).

By virtue of the construction (
20
), we can invoke the Bellman equation to show that

\displaystyle V^{\pi}(s)

\displaystyle=\pi(a_{0}\,|\,s)Q^{\pi}(s,a_{0})+\pi(a_{1}\,|\,s)Q^{\pi}(s,a_{1})+\pi(a_{2}\,|\,s)Q^{\pi}(s,a_{2})

\displaystyle=\pi(a_{1}\,|\,s)\cdot\gamma V^{\pi}(\overline{s-1})+\pi(a_{0}\,|\,s)Q^{\pi}(s,a_{0})+\pi(a_{2}\,|\,s)Q^{\pi}(s,a_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\pi(a_{1}\,|\,s)\gamma^{2s}+\big\{\pi(a_{0}\,|\,s)+\pi(a_{2}\,|\,s)\big\}\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\tau_{s}

\displaystyle=\gamma^{2s}\pi(a_{1}\,|\,s)+\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\tau_{s}\big(1-\pi(a_{1}\,|\,s)\big).

(76)

Here, the second identity comes from (
74b
),
the penultimate line follows from (
59
), (
72
),
as well as the facts
V^{\pi}(\overline{s-1})\leq V^{\star}(\overline{s-1})
,
while the last inequality exploits the fact
\pi(a_{0}\,|\,s)+\pi(a_{2}\,|\,s)=1-\pi(a_{1}\,|\,s)
.

If
V^{\pi}(s)\geq\tau_{s}
,
then this together with the upper bound (
76
) necessarily requires that

\tau_{s}\leq\gamma^{2s}\pi(a_{1}\,|\,s)+\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\tau_{s}\big(1-\pi(a_{1}\,|\,s)\big),

which is equivalent to saying that

\pi(a_{1}\,|\,s)\geq\frac{\tau_{s}-\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\tau_{s}}{\gamma^{2s}-\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\tau_{s}}=\frac{1-\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2\gamma^{\frac{4s}{3}}-\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}}\geq\frac{1-\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\gamma^{\frac{4s}{3}}}=\frac{1-\gamma}{\gamma^{\frac{4s}{3}}(1+\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}})}\geq\frac{1-\gamma}{2}.

(77)

Putting these arguments together establishes the advertised result (
60
).

Proof of Part (iii).

For any
\overline{s}\in\{\overline{3},\cdots,\overline{H}\}
, in view of the construction (
21
) and the Bellman equation, one has

\displaystyle Q^{\pi}(\overline{s},a_{0})

\displaystyle=r(\overline{s},a_{0})+\gamma V^{\pi}(0)=\gamma\tau_{s};

\displaystyle Q^{\pi}(\overline{s},a_{1})

\displaystyle=r(\overline{s},a_{1})+\gamma V^{\pi}(s)=\gamma V^{\pi}(s).

Regarding state
\overline{1}
, we have

\displaystyle Q^{\pi}(\overline{1},a_{0})

\displaystyle=r(\overline{1},a_{0})+\gamma V^{\pi}(0)=\gamma\tau_{1};

\displaystyle Q^{\pi}(\overline{1},a_{1})

\displaystyle=r(\overline{1},a_{1})+\gamma\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}_{1}|}\sum\nolimits_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}V^{\pi}(s^{\prime})=\gamma V^{\pi}(1).

Similarly, one obtains
Q^{\pi}(\overline{2},a_{0})=\gamma\tau_{2}
and
Q^{\pi}(\overline{2},a_{1})=\gamma V^{\pi}(2)
.

Next, let us decompose
V^{\pi}(\overline{s})
as follows:

\displaystyle V^{\pi}(\overline{s})

\displaystyle=\pi(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})Q^{\pi}(\overline{s},a_{0})+\pi(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})Q^{\pi}(\overline{s},a_{1})

\displaystyle=\gamma\tau_{s}\pi(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})+\gamma\pi(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})V^{\pi}(s)=\gamma\tau_{s}+\gamma\pi(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\big(V^{\pi}(s)-\tau_{s}\big),

where we have used
\pi(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})+\pi(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})=1
.
From this relation and the assumption
\pi(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})>0
, it is straightforward to see that
V^{\pi}(\overline{s})\geq\gamma\tau_{s}
if and only if
V^{\pi}(s)\geq\tau_{s}
.
The claim (
63
) regarding
t_{s}(\tau_{s})
and
t_{\overline{s}}(\gamma\tau_{s})
then follows directly from the definition of
t_{s}
(see (
30
) and (
31
)).

Proof of Part (iv).

For any
s_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}
, the Bellman equation yields

\displaystyle Q^{\pi}(s_{1},a_{0})

\displaystyle=r(s_{1},a_{0})+\gamma V^{\pi}(0)=-\gamma^{2}+0=-\gamma^{2},

\displaystyle Q^{\pi}(s_{1},a_{1})

\displaystyle=r(s_{1},a_{1})+\gamma V^{\pi}(0)=\gamma^{2}+0=\gamma^{2},

and hence

V^{\pi}(s_{1})=\pi(a_{0}\,|\,s_{1})Q^{\pi}(s_{1},a_{0})+\pi(a_{1}\,|\,s_{1})Q^{\pi}(s_{1},a_{1})=-\gamma^{2}\pi(a_{0}\,|\,s_{1})+\gamma^{2}\pi(a_{1}\,|\,s_{1}).

A similar argument immediately yields that for any
s_{2}\in\mathcal{S}_{2}
,

Q^{\pi}(s_{2},a_{0})=-\gamma^{4},\qquad Q^{\pi}(s_{2},a_{1})=\gamma^{4},\qquad\text{and}\qquad V^{\pi}(s_{2})=-\gamma^{4}\pi(a_{0}\,|\,s_{2})+\gamma^{4}\pi(a_{1}\,|\,s_{2}).

These together with our notation convention (
25
) establish (
64
).

Proof of Part (v).

Suppose instead that
V^{\pi}(s-1)<\tau_{s-1}
. In view of the basic property (
62
) in Lemma
8
,
this necessarily requires that

\displaystyle V^{\pi}(\overline{s-1})<\gamma\tau_{s-1}.

(78)

Taking (
78
) together with the relation (
59b
) allows us to reach

\displaystyle Q^{\pi}(s,a_{1})=\gamma V^{\pi}(\overline{s-1})<\gamma^{2}\tau_{s-1}=\gamma^{\frac{4}{3}}\tau_{s}.

(79)

In addition, the properties (
59a
) and (
59c
) imply that

\displaystyle Q^{\pi}(s,a_{0})<\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\tau_{s}\qquad\text{and }\qquad Q^{\pi}(s,a_{2})<\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\tau_{s}.

Putting everything together implies that

\displaystyle V^{\pi}(s)

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big\{Q^{\pi}(s,a_{0}),Q^{\pi}(s,a_{1}),Q^{\pi}(s,a_{2})\Big\}<\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\tau_{s},

which contracticts the assumption
V^{\pi}(s)\geq\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\tau_{s}
. This establishes the claimed result for any
s\in\{3,\cdots,H\}
.

Proof of Part (vi).

First, due to explicit expressions of the Q functions (
74a
) and (
74c
), one has

\displaystyle Q^{\pi}(s,a_{0})-Q^{\pi}(s,a_{2})

\displaystyle=\gamma^{2}p\tau_{s-2}-\gamma pV^{\pi}(\overline{s-2})=\gamma p\big(\gamma\tau_{s-2}-V^{\pi}(\overline{s-2})\big)>0,

where the last relation holds since
V^{\pi}(\overline{s-2})<\gamma\tau_{s-2}
when
V^{\pi}(s-2)<\tau_{s-2}

(see (
62
)).

In addition, following the same derivation as for (
79
),
we see that the condition
V^{\pi}(s-1)\leq\tau_{s-1}
implies

\displaystyle Q^{\pi}(s,a_{1})\leq\gamma^{2}\tau_{s-1}.

It is also seen from Part (i) of this lemma that

\displaystyle Q^{\pi}(s,a_{0})\geq\gamma^{3/2}\tau_{s-1}\qquad\text{and}\qquad Q^{\pi}(s,a_{2})\geq\gamma^{3/2}\tau_{s-1},

provided that
V^{\pi}(\overline{s-2})\geq 0
.
Combining these two inequalities, we arrive at the claimed bound

\displaystyle\min\big\{Q^{\pi}(s,a_{0}),Q^{\pi}(s,a_{2})\big\}-Q^{\pi}(s,a_{1})\geq\gamma^{3/2}\tau_{s-1}-\gamma^{2}\tau_{s-1}=\frac{\gamma^{3/2}\left(1-\gamma\right)\tau_{s-1}}{1+\gamma^{1/2}}\geq(1-\gamma)/8,

where the last inequality holds if
\gamma^{2s/3+5/6}\geq\gamma^{s}\geq 1/2
.

Proof of Part (vii).

According to the update rule (
12
), we have — for any policy
\pi
— that

\displaystyle\sum_{a}\frac{\partial V^{\pi_{\theta}}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a)}=

\displaystyle~\sum_{a}\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d^{\pi_{\theta}}_{\mu}(s)\pi_{\theta}(a\,|\,s)\big(Q^{\pi_{\theta}}(s,a)-V^{\pi_{\theta}}(s)\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d^{\pi_{\theta}}_{\mu}(s)\Big(\sum_{a}\pi_{\theta}(a\,|\,s)Q^{\pi_{\theta}}(s,a)-V^{\pi_{\theta}}(s)\sum_{a}\pi_{\theta}(a\,|\,s)\Big)=0,

where we have used the identities
\sum_{a}\pi_{\theta}(a\,|\,s)=1
and
V^{\pi}(s)=\sum_{a}\pi(a\,|\,s)Q^{\pi}(s,a)
.
As a result, if
\sum_{a}\theta^{(0)}(s,a)=0
, then it follows from the PG update rule that
\sum_{a}\theta^{(t)}(s,a)=0
.

A.5
Proof of Lemma
11

Proof of Part (i).

Dividing both sides of (
67a
) by
x_{t}x_{t-1}
,
we obtain

\frac{1}{x_{t-1}}\geq\frac{1}{x_{t}}-\frac{c_{\mathrm{l}}x_{t-1}}{x_{t}}.

If
c_{1}x_{0}\leq 1/2
, then the monotonicity assumption gives
c_{\mathrm{l}}x_{t}\leq 1/2

for all
t\geq 0
. It then follows that

\frac{x_{t}}{x_{t-1}}\geq 1-c_{\mathrm{l}}x_{t-1}\geq\frac{1}{2}\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad\frac{1}{x_{t-1}}\geq\frac{1}{x_{t}}-\frac{c_{\mathrm{l}}x_{t-1}}{x_{t}}\geq\frac{1}{x_{t}}-2c_{\mathrm{l}}.

Apply this relation recursively to deduce that

\frac{1}{x_{t}}\leq\frac{1}{x_{t-1}}+2c_{\mathrm{l}}\leq\cdots\leq\frac{1}{x_{0}}+2c_{\mathrm{l}}t.

This readily concludes the proof of (
67b
).

Proof of Part (ii).

Similarly, divide both sides of (
68a
)
by
x_{t}x_{t-1}
to derive

\frac{1}{x_{t-1}}\leq\frac{1}{x_{t}}-\frac{c_{\mathrm{u}}x_{t-1}}{x_{t}}\leq\frac{1}{x_{t}}-c_{\mathrm{u}},

given the monotonicity and positivity assumption
0<x_{t}\leq x_{t-1}
. Invoking
this inequality recursively gives

\frac{1}{x_{t}}\geq\frac{1}{x_{t-1}}+c_{\mathrm{u}}\geq\cdots\geq\frac{1}{x_{0}}+c_{\mathrm{u}}t,

thus establishing the advertised bound (
68b
).

Proof of Part (iii).

We now turn attention to (
69b
).
As is clearly seen, the non-negative sequence
\{x_{t}\}
majorizes another sequence
\{y_{t}\}
generated as follows (in the sense that
x_{t}\geq y_{t}
for all
0<t\leq t_{0}
)

y_{0}=x_{0}\qquad\text{and}\qquad y_{t}=y_{t-1}+c_{-}y_{t-1}^{2}\quad\text{for all }0<t\leq t_{0}

(80)

Dividing both sides of the second equation of (
80
)
by
y_{t-1}y_{t}
, we reach

\frac{1}{y_{t-1}}=\frac{1}{y_{t}}+c_{-}\frac{y_{t-1}}{y_{t}}\geq\frac{1}{y_{t}}+\frac{c_{-}}{1+c_{-}c_{x}}.

To see why the last inequality holds, note that, according to the
first equation of (
80
) and the
assumption
x_{t-1}<c_{x}
(and hence
y_{t-1}\leq x_{t-1}<c_{x}
), we have

\frac{y_{t}}{y_{t-1}}=1+c_{-}y_{t-1}\leq 1+c_{-}c_{x}.

As a result, we can apply the preceding inequalities recursively to
derive

\frac{1}{y_{0}}\geq\frac{1}{y_{1}}+\frac{c_{-}}{1+c_{-}c_{x}}\geq\cdots\geq\frac{1}{y_{t_{0}}}+\frac{c_{-}}{1+c_{-}c_{x}}t_{0}\geq\frac{c_{-}}{1+c_{-}c_{x}}t_{0},

and hence we arrive at (
69b
),

t_{0}\leq\frac{1+c_{-}c_{x}}{c_{-}y_{0}}=\frac{1+c_{-}c_{x}}{c_{-}x_{0}}.

Proof of Part (iv).

The proof of (
70b
) is quite similar to that of (
69b
).
Let us construct another non-negative sequence
\{z_{t}\}
as follows

z_{0}=x_{0}\qquad\text{and}\qquad z_{t}=z_{t-1}+c_{+}z_{t-1}^{2}\quad\text{for all }0<t\leq t_{0}.

(81)

Comparing this with (
70a
) clearly reveals that
z_{t}\geq x_{t}
.
Divide both sides of (
81
) by
z_{t}z_{t-1}
to reach

\frac{1}{z_{t-1}}=\frac{1}{z_{t}}+c_{+}\frac{z_{t-1}}{z_{t}}\leq\frac{1}{z_{t}}+c_{+},

where the last inequality is valid since, by construction,
z_{t}\geq z_{t-1}
.
Applying this relation recursively yields

\frac{1}{z_{0}}\leq\frac{1}{z_{t_{0}}}+c_{+}t_{0},

which taken together with the fact
z_{0}=x_{0}
and
z_{t_{0}}\geq x_{t_{0}}
leads to

t_{0}\geq\frac{\frac{1}{z_{0}}-\frac{1}{z_{t_{0}}}}{c_{+}}\geq\frac{\frac{1}{x_{0}}-\frac{1}{x_{t_{0}}}}{c_{+}}.

Appendix B
Discounted state visitation probability (Lemmas
2
-
3
)

In this section, we establish our bounds concerning the discounted state visitation probability,
as claimed in Lemma
2
and Lemma
3
.
Throughout this section, we denote by
\mathbb{P}(\cdot\,|\,\pi)
the probability distribution when policy
\pi
is adopted.
Also, we recall that
\mu
is taken to be a uniform distribution over all states.

B.1
Lower bounds: proof of Lemma
2

Consider an arbitrary policy
\pi
, and let
\{s^{k}\}_{k\geq 0}
represent an MDP trajectory.
For any
s\in\{3,\cdots,H\}
, it follows from the definition (
13
) of
d_{\mu}^{\pi}
that

\displaystyle d_{\mu}^{\pi}(s)

\displaystyle=(1-\gamma)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{k}\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi\big)

(82)

\displaystyle\geq(1-\gamma)\gamma\mathbb{P}\big(s^{1}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi\big)\geq(1-\gamma)\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{s}}\mathbb{P}\big(s^{1}=s\,|\,s^{0}=s^{\prime},\pi\big)\mathbb{P}(s_{0}=s^{\prime}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu)

\displaystyle=(1-\gamma)\gamma\cdot\frac{|\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{s}|}{|\mathcal{S}|}=c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)^{2}.

Here, the penultimate identity is valid due to the construction (
24
) and the assumption that
\mu
is uniformly distributed,
whereas the last identity results from the assumption (
17
). This establishes (
36a
). Repeating the same argument also reveals that

\displaystyle d_{\mu}^{\pi}(\overline{s})\geq c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)^{2}

for any
\overline{s}\in\{\overline{1},\cdots,\overline{H}\}
, thus validating the lower bound (
36b
).

In addition, for any
s\in\mathcal{S}_{1}
, the MDP construction (
24
) allows one to derive

\displaystyle d_{\mu}^{\pi}(s)

\displaystyle=(1-\gamma)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{k}\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi\big)\geq\gamma(1-\gamma)\mathbb{P}\big(s^{1}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi\big)

\displaystyle\geq\gamma(1-\gamma)\mathbb{P}\big(s^{1}=s\,|\,s^{0}\in\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{1}\big)\,\mathbb{P}\big(s_{0}\in\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{1}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\gamma(1-\gamma)\cdot\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}_{1}|}\cdot\frac{|\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{1}|}{|\mathcal{S}|}=\gamma(1-\gamma)\frac{c_{\mathrm{m}}}{c_{\mathrm{b},1}}\cdot\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}.

Here, the last line holds due to the fact that
\mu
is uniformly distributed and the assumptions (
16
) and (
17
).
We have thus concluded the proof for (
36c
). The proof for (
36d
) follows from an identical argument and is hence omitted.

B.2
Upper bounds: proof of Lemma
3

B.2.1
Preliminary facts

Before embarking on the proof, we collect several basic yet useful properties that happen when
t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
. The first-time readers can proceed directly to Appendix
B.2.2
.

Properties about
{Q}^{(t)}(\overline{s},a)
.

Combine the property (
61
) in Lemma
8
with (
32
) to yield that: for any
1\leq s\leq H
and any
t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
, one has

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})=\gamma V^{(t)}(s)<\gamma{\tau}_{s}=Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{0})

(83)

In addition, combining the property (
61
) in Lemma
8
with (
66
) yields: for any
2\leq s\leq H
,

\displaystyle V^{(t)}(\overline{s^{\prime}})\leq\max\Big\{Q^{(t)}\big(\overline{s^{\prime}},a_{0}\big),Q^{(t)}\big(\overline{s^{\prime}},a_{1}\big)\Big\}=\max\Big\{\gamma\tau_{s^{\prime}},\gamma V^{(t)}(s^{\prime})\Big\}=\gamma\tau_{s^{\prime}}

(84)

holds for all
s^{\prime}
obeying
s\leq s^{\prime}\leq H
and all
t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
.
As a remark, (
83
) indicates that
a_{1}
remains unfavored (according to the current estimate
Q^{(t)}
)
before the iteration number hits
t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
.

Properties about
{Q}^{(t)}(s+1,a)
and
{Q}^{(t)}(s+2,a)
.

First, combining (
84
) with the relation (
74
) reveals that: for any
2\leq s\leq H-1
and any
t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
,

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s+1,a_{0})=r_{s+1}+\gamma^{2}p\tau_{s-1}\geq r_{s+1},

(85a)

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s+1,a_{1})=\gamma V^{(t)}(\overline{s})\leq\gamma^{2}\tau_{s}=\gamma^{1/2}r_{s+1},

(85b)

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s+1,a_{2})=r_{s+1}+\gamma pV^{(t)}(\overline{s-1})\geq r_{s+1}

(85c)

hold as long as
V^{(t)}(\overline{s-1})\geq 0
(which is guaranteed by Lemma
10
).
Similarly, (
84
) and (
74
) also give

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s+2,a_{0})=r_{s+2}+\gamma^{2}p\tau_{s}

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s+2,a_{1})=\gamma V^{(t)}(\overline{s+1})=\gamma^{2}\tau_{s+1}=\gamma^{1/2}r_{s+2},

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s+2,a_{2})=r_{s+2}+\gamma pV^{(t)}(\overline{s})\leq r_{s+2}+\gamma^{2}p\tau_{s}

for any
1\leq s\leq H-2
and any
t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
.
Consequently, we have

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s+1,a_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\min\big\{Q^{(t)}(s+1,a_{0}),Q^{(t)}(s+1,a_{2})\big\},\quad

\displaystyle\text{if }2\leq s\leq H-1

(86a)

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s+2,a_{2})

\displaystyle\leq Q^{(t)}(s+2,a_{0}),\quad

\displaystyle\text{if }1\leq s\leq H-2

(86b)

for all
t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
.
In other words, the above two inequalities reveal that actions
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
are perceived as suboptimal (based on the current Q-function estimates) before the iteration count surpasses
t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
.

Next, consider any
2\leq s\leq H-1
and any
t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
.
It has already been shown above that

\displaystyle{Q}^{(t)}(s+1,a)\geq{Q}^{(t)}(s+1,a_{1}),\qquad a\in\{a_{0},a_{2}\}.

(87a)

A similar argument also implies that, for any
t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
,

\displaystyle{Q}^{(t)}(s+2,a_{0})\geq{Q}^{(t)}(s+2,a_{2}),

(87b)

which forms another property useful for our subsequent analysis.

B.2.2
Proof of the upper bounds (
37a
) and (
37b
)

We now turn attention to upper bounding
d_{\mu}^{(t)}(s)
for any
s\in\{3,\cdots,H\}
.
By virtue of the expansion (
82
),
upper bounding
d_{\mu}^{(t)}(s)
requires controlling
\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)
for all
k\geq 0
.
In light of this, our analysis consists of (i) developing upper bounds on the inter-related quantities
\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)
and
\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=\overline{s}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)
for any
k\geq 0
, and (ii) combining these upper bounds to control
d_{\mu}^{(t)}(s)
.
At the core of our analysis is the following upper bounds on the
t
-th policy iterate, which will be established in Appendix
B.2.6
.

Lemma 12
.

Under the assumption (
35
), for any
2\leq s\leq H
and any
t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
, one has

\displaystyle\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\leq\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\leq 1/2.

(88a)

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s+1)\leq\min\big\{\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s+1),\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s+1)\big\}\quad\text{and}\quad\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s+1)\leq 1/3

(88b)

hold if
2\leq s\leq H-1
, and

\displaystyle\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s+2)\leq\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s+2)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s+2)\leq 1/2

(88c)

hold if
1\leq s\leq H-2
.

In words, Lemma
12
posits that, at the beginning, the policy iterate
\pi^{(t)}
does not assign too much probability mass on actions that are currently perceived as suboptimal (see the remarks in Appendix
B.2.1
).
With this lemma in place, we are positioned to establish the advertised upper bound.

Step 1: bounding
\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)
.

For any
t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
and any
s\in\{3,\cdots,H\}
, making use of the upper bound (
88a
) and the MDP construction in Section
3
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(s^{0}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle=1/{|\mathcal{S}|},

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(s^{1}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(s^{0}\in\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{s}\big)+\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\,\mathbb{P}(s^{0}=\overline{s})\leq\frac{|\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{s}|}{|\mathcal{S}|}+\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}\leq\frac{2|\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{s}|}{|\mathcal{S}|}=2c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle=\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\,\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k-1}=\overline{s}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k-1}=\overline{s}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

for all
k\geq 2
. Note that the above calculation exploits the fact that
\mu
is a uniform distribution.

Step 2: bounding
\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=\overline{s}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)
.

Given that
\mu
is a uniform distribution, one has

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(s^{0}=\overline{s}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle=1/|\mathcal{S}|

(90a)

for any
s\in\mathcal{S}
.
With (
88b
) and (
88c
) in mind,
the MDP construction in Section
3
allows one to show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(s^{1}=\overline{s}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(s^{0}\in\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{\overline{s}}\big)+\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s+1)\,\mathbb{P}(s^{0}=s+1)+\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s+2)\,\mathbb{P}(s^{0}=s+2)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{\overline{s}}|}{|\mathcal{S}|}+\frac{1}{3|\mathcal{S}|}+\frac{1}{2|\mathcal{S}|}\leq\frac{2|\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{\overline{s}}|}{|\mathcal{S}|}=2c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)

(90b)

holds for any
2\leq s\leq H-2
and any
t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
, and in addition,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=\overline{s}\,|\,s_{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s+1)\,\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k-1}=s+1\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)+\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s+2)\,\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k-1}=s+2\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k-1}=s+1\,|\,s_{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k-1}=s+2\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

(90c)

hold for any
k\geq 2
,
2\leq s\leq H-2
, and any
t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
.
Moreover, invoking (
88b
) and the MDP construction once again reveals that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=\overline{H-1}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,H)\,\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k-1}=H\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)\leq\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k-1}=H\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=\overline{H}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle=0

hold for any
k\geq 2
and any
t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
. In addition, it is seen that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(s^{1}=\overline{H-1}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(s^{0}\in\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{\overline{H-1}}\big)+\mathbb{P}(s^{0}=H)=\frac{\big|\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{\overline{H-1}}\big|}{|\mathcal{S}|}+\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}\leq\frac{2\big|\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{\overline{H-1}}\big|}{|\mathcal{S}|}=2c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(s^{1}=\overline{H}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(s^{0}\in\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{\overline{H}}\big)=\frac{\big|\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{\overline{H}}\big|}{|\mathcal{S}|}=c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma).

Step 3: putting all this together.

Combining the preceding upper bounds on both
\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)
and
\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=\overline{s}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

(k\geq 1
) and recognizing the monotonicity property (
33
), we immediately arrive at the following crude bounds

\displaystyle\max_{3\leq s\leq H,t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})}\Big\{\mathbb{P}\big(s^{0}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big),\mathbb{P}\big(s^{1}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq 1/|\mathcal{S}|\leq 2c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)

\displaystyle\max_{2\leq s\leq H,t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})}\Big\{\mathbb{P}\big(s^{0}=\overline{s}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big),\mathbb{P}\big(s^{1}=\overline{s}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq 2c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)

\displaystyle\max_{3\leq s\leq H,t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})}\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5}{6}\max_{2\leq s\leq H,t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})}\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k-1}=\overline{s}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\max_{2\leq s\leq H,t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})}\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=\overline{s}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5}{6}\max_{3\leq s\leq H,t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})}\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k-1}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

for any
k\geq 2
. It is then straightforward to deduce that

\displaystyle\max_{3\leq s\leq H,t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})}\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{5}{6}\right)^{k-1}2c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)

(91a)

\displaystyle\max_{2\leq s\leq H,t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})}\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=\overline{s}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{5}{6}\right)^{k-1}2c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)

(91b)

for any
k\geq 1
. In turn, these bounds give rise to

\displaystyle d_{\mu}^{(t)}(s)

\displaystyle=(1-\gamma)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{k}\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)\leq(1-\gamma)\left\{2c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{5}{6}\right)^{k-1}2c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)^{2}+\frac{1}{1-5/6}\cdot 2c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)^{2}=14c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)^{2}

(92a)

for any
3\leq s\leq H
and any
t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
.
This establishes the claimed upper bound (
37a
) as long as Lemma
12
is valid.
Further, replacing
s
with
\overline{s}
in (
92
) also reveals that

\displaystyle d_{\mu}^{(t)}(\overline{s})\leq 14c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)^{2}

(92b)

for any
2\leq s\leq H
and any
t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
, thus concluding the proof of (
37b
).

B.2.3
Proof of the upper bound (
37c
)

We now consider any
s\in\mathcal{S}_{2}
. From our MDP construction, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(s^{0}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle=1/|\mathcal{S}|,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(s^{1}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(s^{1}=s\,|\,s^{0}\in\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{2},\pi^{(t)}\big)\mathbb{P}\big(s^{0}\in\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{2}\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(s^{1}=s\,|\,s^{0}=\overline{2},\pi^{(t)}\big)\,\mathbb{P}\big(s^{0}=\overline{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}_{2}|}\frac{|\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{2}|}{|\mathcal{S}|}+\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}_{2}|}\,\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}\leq\frac{2|\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{2}|}{|\mathcal{S}_{2}|\,|\mathcal{S}|}=\frac{2c_{\mathrm{m}}}{c_{\mathrm{b},2}|\mathcal{S}|},

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=s\,|\,s^{k-1}=\overline{2},\pi^{(t)}\big)\,\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k-1}=\overline{2}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2|\mathcal{S}_{2}|}\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k-1}=\overline{2}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

for any
k\geq 2
and any
s\in\mathcal{S}_{2}
.
In addition, our bound in (
91b
) gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k-1}=\overline{2}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)\leq\left(\frac{5}{6}\right)^{k-2}2c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)

for any
k\geq 2
and any
t<t_{2}({\tau}_{2})
.
Consequently, we arrive at

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}_{2}|}\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k-1}=\overline{2}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)\leq\frac{c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)}{|\mathcal{S}_{2}|}\left(\frac{5}{6}\right)^{k-2}=\frac{c_{\mathrm{m}}}{c_{\mathrm{b},2}|\mathcal{S}|}\left(\frac{5}{6}\right)^{k-2}.

(94)

Armed with the preceding inequalities, we can derive

\displaystyle d_{\mu}^{(t)}(s)

\displaystyle=(1-\gamma)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{k}\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq(1-\gamma)\left\{\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}+\gamma\cdot\frac{2c_{\mathrm{m}}}{c_{\mathrm{b},2}|\mathcal{S}|}+\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\gamma^{k}\frac{c_{\mathrm{m}}}{c_{\mathrm{b},2}|\mathcal{S}|}\left(\frac{5}{6}\right)^{k-2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1-\gamma}{|\mathcal{S}|}\left(1+\frac{2c_{\mathrm{m}}}{c_{\mathrm{b},2}}\right)+\frac{c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)}{(1-5/6)c_{\mathrm{b},2}|\mathcal{S}|}=\frac{1-\gamma}{|\mathcal{S}|}\left(1+\frac{8c_{\mathrm{m}}}{c_{\mathrm{b},2}}\right)

for any
s\in\mathcal{S}_{2}
and any
t<t_{2}({\tau}_{2})
, thus concluding the advertised upper bound for
s\in\mathcal{S}_{2}
.

B.2.4
Proof of the upper bound (
37d
)

It follows from our MDP construction that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(s^{0}=\overline{1}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle=1/|\mathcal{S}|,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(s^{1}=\overline{1}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(s^{0}\in\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{\overline{1}}\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(s^{0}=3\big)=\frac{|\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{\overline{1}}|}{|\mathcal{S}|}+\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}.

Moreover, for any
k\geq 2
and any
t<t_{3}({\tau}_{3})
, one can derive

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=\overline{1}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle=\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,3)\,\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k-1}=3\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)\leq\left(\frac{5}{6}\right)^{k-2}2c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma),

(95)

where the last inequality arises from (
91a
). Putting these bounds together leads to

\displaystyle d_{\mu}^{(t)}(\overline{1})

\displaystyle=(1-\gamma)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{k}\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=\overline{1}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)\leq(1-\gamma)\left\{\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}+\gamma\left(\frac{|\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{\overline{1}}|}{|\mathcal{S}|}+\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}\right)+\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\left(\frac{5}{6}\right)^{k-2}2c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\gamma)\left\{\frac{2|\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{\overline{1}}|}{|\mathcal{S}|}+\frac{1}{1-5/6}2c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)\right\}=14c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)^{2},

where we have used the assumption that
|\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{\overline{1}}|=c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)|\mathcal{S}|
.
When
t<t_{2}({\tau}_{2})
, the monotonicity property (
33
) indicates that
t<t_{3}({\tau}_{3})
, thus concluding the proof of (
37d
).

B.2.5
Proof of the upper bound (
37e
)

In view of our MDP construction, for any
s\in\mathcal{S}_{1}
and any
t<\min\{t_{1}({\tau}_{1}),t_{2}({\tau}_{2})\}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(s^{0}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle=1/|\mathcal{S}|,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(s^{1}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(s^{1}=s\,|\,s^{0}\in\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{1},\pi^{(t)}\big)\mathbb{P}\big(s^{0}\in\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{1}\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(s^{1}=s\,|\,s^{0}=\overline{1},\pi^{(t)}\big)\,\mathbb{P}\big(s^{0}=\overline{1}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}_{1}|}\frac{|\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{1}|}{|\mathcal{S}|}+\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}_{1}|}\,\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}\leq\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}_{1}|}\frac{2|\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{1}|}{|\mathcal{S}|}=\frac{2c_{\mathrm{m}}}{c_{\mathrm{b},1}|\mathcal{S}|},

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=s\,|\,s^{k-1}=\overline{1},\pi^{(t)}\big)\,\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k-1}=\overline{1}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}_{1}|}\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k-1}=\overline{1}\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)\leq\frac{2c_{\mathrm{m}}}{c_{\mathrm{b},1}|\mathcal{S}|}\left(\frac{5}{6}\right)^{k-3},

where
k
is any integer obeying
k\geq 2
.
Here, the last inequality comes from (
95
).
These bounds taken collectively demonstrate that

\displaystyle d_{\mu}^{(t)}(s)

\displaystyle=(1-\gamma)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{k}\mathbb{P}\big(s^{k}=s\,|\,s^{0}\sim\mu,\pi^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq(1-\gamma)\left\{\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}+\gamma\cdot\frac{2c_{\mathrm{m}}}{c_{\mathrm{b},1}|\mathcal{S}|}+\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\gamma^{k}\frac{2c_{\mathrm{m}}}{c_{\mathrm{b},1}|\mathcal{S}|}\left(\frac{5}{6}\right)^{k-3}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1-\gamma}{|\mathcal{S}|}\left(1+\frac{2c_{\mathrm{m}}}{c_{\mathrm{b},1}}\right)+\frac{\frac{6}{5}\cdot 2c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)}{(1-5/6)c_{\mathrm{b},1}|\mathcal{S}|}\leq\frac{1-\gamma}{|\mathcal{S}|}\left(1+\frac{17c_{\mathrm{m}}}{c_{\mathrm{b},1}}\right)

for any
s\in\mathcal{S}_{1}
and any
t<\min\{t_{1}({\tau}_{1}),t_{2}({\tau}_{2})\}
. This completes the proof.

B.2.6
Proof of Lemma
12

In order to prove this lemma, we are in need of the following auxiliary result, whose proof can be found in Appendix
B.2.7
.

Lemma 13
.

Consider any state
1\leq s\leq H
. Suppose that
0<\eta\leq(1-\gamma)/2
.

(i)

If the following conditions

\displaystyle{Q}^{(t)}(s,a_{0})

\displaystyle-{Q}^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\geq 0,\quad\quad\quad{Q}^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-{Q}^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\geq 0

\displaystyle\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\leq\min\big\{\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{0}\,|\,s),\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\big\}

hold, then one has
\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\leq 1/3
and
\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\leq\min\big\{\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s),\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\big\}
.

(ii)

If the following conditions

\displaystyle{Q}^{(t)}(s,a_{0})-{Q}^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\geq 0\quad\text{and}\quad\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\leq\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)

hold, then one has
\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\leq 1/2
and
\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\leq\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)
.

(iii)

If the following conditions

\displaystyle{Q}^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{0})-{Q}^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})\geq 0\quad\text{and}\quad\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\leq\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})

hold, then one has
\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\leq 1/2
and
\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\leq\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})
.

Remark
9
.

In words, Lemma
13
develops nontrivial upper bounds on the policy associated with actions that are currently perceived as suboptimal. As we shall see, such upper bounds — which are strictly below 1 — translate to some contraction factors that enable the advertised result of this lemma.

With Lemma
13
in place, we proceed to prove Lemma
12
by induction.
Let us start from the base case with
t=0
.
Given that the initial policy is chosen to be uniformly distributed, we have

\displaystyle\pi^{(0)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)

\displaystyle=\pi^{(0)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)=\pi^{(0)}(a_{2}\,|\,s),

\displaystyle 3\leq s\leq H;

\displaystyle\pi^{(0)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})

\displaystyle=\pi^{(0)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s}),

\displaystyle 1\leq s\leq H.

Therefore, the claim (
88
) trivially holds for
t=0
.

Next, we move on to the induction step.
Suppose that the induction hypothesis (
88
) holds for the
t
-th iteration,
and we intend to establish it for the
(t+1)
-th iteration.
Apply Lemma
13
with Conditions (
83
) and (
88a
) to yield

\pi^{(t+1)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\leq\pi^{(t+1)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})

with the proviso that
0<\eta\leq(1-\gamma)/2
. Clearly, this also implies that
\pi^{(t+1)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\leq 1/2
.
Further, invoke Lemma
13
once again with Condition (
87
) and the induction hypothesis (
88
)
to arrive at

\displaystyle\pi^{(t+1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s+1)\leq\min\big\{\pi^{(t+1)}(a_{0}\,|\,s+1),\pi^{(t+1)}(a_{2}\,|\,s+1)\big\},

\displaystyle\text{if }2\leq s\leq H-1;

\displaystyle\pi^{(t+1)}(a_{2}\,|\,s+2)\leq\pi^{(t+1)}(a_{0}\,|\,s+2),

\displaystyle\text{if }1\leq s\leq H-2.

A straightforward consequence is
\pi^{(t+1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s+1)\leq 1/3
and
\pi^{(t+1)}(a_{2}\,|\,s+2)\leq 1/2
.
The proof is thus complete by induction.

B.2.7
Proof of Lemma
13

First of all, suppose that
{Q}^{(t)}(s,a_{0})-{Q}^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\geq 0
and
\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\geq\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)
hold true.
Combining this result with the PG update rule (
12
) gives

\displaystyle\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})

\displaystyle=\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})+\frac{\eta}{1-\gamma}d_{\mu}^{(t-1)}(s)\,\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\,{A}^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})+\frac{\eta}{1-\gamma}d_{\mu}^{(t-1)}(s)\,\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\,{A}^{(t-1)}(s,a_{0}).

Consequently, applying this inequality and using the PG update rule (
12
) yield

\displaystyle\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})+\frac{\eta}{1-\gamma}d_{\mu}^{(t-1)}(s)\,\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\,{A}^{(t-1)}(s,a_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\qquad-\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{0})-\frac{\eta}{1-\gamma}d_{\mu}^{(t-1)}(s)\,\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\,{A}^{(t-1)}(s,a_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\Big\{\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{0})\Big\}+\Big\{\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)-\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\Big\}\,\Big|\frac{\eta}{1-\gamma}d_{\mu}^{(t-1)}(s){A}^{(t-1)}(s,a_{0})\Big|,

(97)

where the last line arises by combining terms and invoking the assumption
\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\geq\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)
.

Additionally, it is seen from the definition of the advantage function that

\big|A^{(t-1)}(s,a_{0})\big|\leq\max_{\pi,a}\big|Q^{\pi}(s,a)\big|+\max_{\pi}\big|V^{\pi}(s)\big|\leq 2,

(98)

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
1
.
Recognizing that
d^{(t-1)}_{\mu}(s)\leq 1
, one obtains

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\eta}{1-\gamma}d_{\mu}^{(t-1)}(s)A^{(t-1)}(s,a_{0})\Big|\leq\frac{\eta}{1-\gamma}\cdot 2\leq 1,

(99)

with the proviso that
0<\eta\leq(1-\gamma)/2
.

Substituting (
99
) into (
97
) then yields

(
97
)

\displaystyle\leq\Big\{\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{0})\Big\}+\Big\{\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)-\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\Big\{\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{0})\Big\}-\Big\{\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{0})-\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})\Big\}=0,

(100)

where both the first line and the last identity rely on the fact that
\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})\leq\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{0})
— an immediate consequence of the assumption
\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\leq\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)
.
To see why the inequality (
100
) holds, it suffices to make note of the following consequence of softmax parameterization:

\displaystyle\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)-\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)

\displaystyle=\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\Big\{\exp\big[\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{0})-\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})\big]-1\Big\}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(a)}}{\leq}\frac{\exp\big[\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{0})-\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})\big]-1}{\exp\big[\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{0})-\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})\big]+1}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(b)}}{\leq}\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{0})-\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1}),

where (b) follows since
\frac{e^{x}-1}{e^{x}+1}\leq x
for all
x\geq 0
, and the validity of (a) is guaranteed since

\displaystyle\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp\big(\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})\big)}{\sum_{a}\exp\big(\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a)\big)}\leq\frac{\exp\big(\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})\big)}{\exp\big(\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{0})\big)+\exp\big(\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})\big)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\exp\big[\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{0})-\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})\big]+1}.

To conclude, the above result (
100
) implies that

\displaystyle\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\geq\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s).

(101)

Repeating the above argument immediately reveals that: if

Q^{(t-1)}(s,a_{2})\geq Q^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})\quad\text{and}\quad\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\geq\pi^{(t-1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s),

then one has
\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\geq\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)
, which together with (
101
) indicates that

\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\leq\min\big\{\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s),\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\big\}

\Longrightarrow\qquad\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\leq\frac{\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)+\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)+\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)}{3}=\frac{1}{3}.

This establishes Part (i) of Lemma
13
.

The proofs of Parts (ii) and (iii) follow from exactly the same argument as for Part (i), and are hence omitted for the sake of brevity.

Appendix C
Crossing times of the first few states (Lemma
4
)

This section presents the proof of Lemma
4
regarding the crossing times w.r.t.
\mathcal{S}_{1}
,
\mathcal{S}_{2}
, and state
\overline{1}
.

C.1
Crossing times for the buffer states in
\mathcal{S}_{1}
and
\mathcal{S}_{2}

We first present the proof of the relation (
38a
) regarding several quantities about
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
.

Step 1: characterize the policy gradients.

Our analysis largely relies on understanding the policy gradient dynamics, towards which we need to first characterize the gradient.
Recalling that the gradient of
V^{(t)}
w.r.t.
\theta_{t}(1,a_{1})
(cf. (
12b
)) is given by

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(1,a_{1})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d^{(t)}_{\mu}(1)\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,1)\Big\{Q^{(t)}(1,a_{1})-V^{(t)}(1)\Big\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d^{(t)}_{\mu}(1)\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,1)\Big\{Q^{(t)}(1,a_{1})-\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,1)Q^{(t)}(1,a_{0})-\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,1)Q^{(t)}(1,a_{1})\Big\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d^{(t)}_{\mu}(1)\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,1)\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,1)\Big\{Q^{(t)}(1,a_{1})-Q^{(t)}(1,a_{0})\Big\}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\gamma^{2}}{1-\gamma}d^{(t)}_{\mu}(1)\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,1)\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,1)>0,

(102)

where in the last step we use
Q^{(t)}(1,a_{1})-Q^{(t)}(1,a_{0})=2\gamma^{2}
(see (
64
)).
The same calculation also yields

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(2,a_{1})}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\gamma^{4}}{1-\gamma}d^{(t)}_{\mu}(2)\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,2)\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,2)>0.

(103)

As an immediate consequence, the PG update rule (
12a
) reveals that
both
\theta^{(t)}(1,a_{1})
(resp.
\pi^{(t)}(1,a_{1})
) and
\theta^{(t)}(2,a_{1})
(resp.
\pi^{(t)}(2,a_{1})
)
are monotonically increasing with
t
throughout the execution of the algorithm,
which together with the initial condition
\pi^{(0)}(a_{0}\,|\,1)=\pi^{(0)}(a_{1}\,|\,1)=\pi^{(0)}(a_{0}\,|\,2)=\pi^{(0)}(a_{1}\,|\,2)

as well as the identities
\theta^{(t)}(1,a_{1})=-\theta^{(t)}(1,a_{0})
and
\theta^{(t)}(2,a_{1})=-\theta^{(t)}(2,a_{0})
(due to (
65
)) gives

\displaystyle\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,1)\leq\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,1)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,2)\leq\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,2)\qquad\text{for all }t\geq 0.

(104)

Step 2: determine the range of
\pi^{(t)}(\cdot\,|\,1)
and
\pi^{(t)}(\cdot\,|\,2)
.

From the basic property (
64
), the value function of the buffer states in
\mathcal{S}_{1}
— abbreviated by
V^{(t)}(1)
as in the notation convention (
25
) — satisfies

\displaystyle V^{(t)}(1)=-\gamma^{2}\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,1)+\gamma^{2}\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,1)=-\gamma^{2}+2\gamma^{2}\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,1),

(105)

given that
\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,1)+\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,1)=1
.
Therefore, for any
t<t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)
— which means
V^{(t)}(1)<\gamma^{2}-1/4
according to the definition (
31
) — one has the following upper bound:

\displaystyle V^{(t)}(1)=-\gamma^{2}+2\gamma^{2}\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,1)<\gamma^{2}-1/4.

This is equivalent to requiring that

\displaystyle\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,1)<1-(8\gamma^{2})^{-1}\leq 7/8

(106)

and, consequently,
\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,1)=1-\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,1)\geq 1/8
for any
t<t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)
.
Putting this and (
104
) together further implies — for every
t<t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)
— that:

\displaystyle 1/8\leq\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,1)\leq\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,1)\leq 7/8.

(107)

Step 3: determine the range of policy gradients.

In addition to showing the non-negativity of
\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(1,a_{1})}
and
\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(2,a_{1})}
for all
t\geq 0
,
we are also in need of bounding their magnitudes.
Towards this, invoke the property (
107
) to bound the derivative (
102
) by

\displaystyle\frac{7\gamma^{2}}{32(1-\gamma)}d^{(t)}_{\mu}(1)\leq\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(1,a_{1})}\leq\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2(1-\gamma)}d^{(t)}_{\mu}(1)

(108)

for any
t<t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)
, where we have used the elementary facts

\min_{1/8\leq x\leq 7/8}x(1-x)=7/64\qquad\text{and}\qquad\max_{0\leq x\leq 1}x(1-x)=1/4.

Similarly, repeating the above argument with the gradient expression (
103
) leads to

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(2,a_{1})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\gamma^{4}}{2(1-\gamma)}d^{(t)}_{\mu}(2)

\displaystyle\text{for all }t\geq 0;

(109a)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(2,a_{1})}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{7\gamma^{4}}{32(1-\gamma)}d^{(t)}_{\mu}(2)

\displaystyle\text{for all }0\leq t<t_{2}(\gamma^{4}-1/4).

(109b)

Further, note that Lemma
2
and Lemma
3
deliver upper and lower bounds on the quantities
d^{(t)}_{\mu}(1)
and
d^{(t)}_{\mu}(2)
,
which allow us to deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(1,a_{1})}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{7\gamma^{3}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{32c_{\mathrm{b},1}|\mathcal{S}|}

\displaystyle\text{for all }t<t_{1}\big(\gamma^{2}-1/4\big);

(110a)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(2,a_{1})}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{7\gamma^{5}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{32c_{\mathrm{b},2}|\mathcal{S}|}

\displaystyle\text{for all }t<t_{2}(\gamma^{4}-1/4);

(110b)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(1,a_{1})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\gamma^{2}(1+17c_{\mathrm{m}}/c_{\mathrm{b},1})}{2|\mathcal{S}|}

\displaystyle\text{for all }t<\min\big\{t_{1}(\tau_{1}),t_{2}(\tau_{2})\big\};

(110c)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(2,a_{1})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\gamma^{4}(1+8c_{\mathrm{m}}/c_{\mathrm{b},2})}{2|\mathcal{S}|}

\displaystyle\text{for all }t<t_{2}(\tau_{2}).

(110d)

Step 4: develop an upper bound on
t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)
.

The preceding bounds allow us to develop an upper bound on
t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)
.
To do so, it is first observed from the fact
\theta^{(t)}(1,a_{0})=-\theta^{(t)}(1,a_{1})
(due to (
65
)) that

\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,1)=\frac{\exp\left(\theta^{(t)}(1,a_{1})\right)}{\exp\left(\theta^{(t)}(1,a_{0})\right)+\exp\left(\theta^{(t)}(1,a_{1})\right)}=1-\frac{1}{1+\exp\left(2\theta^{(t)}(1,a_{1})\right)}.

Recognizing that
V^{(t)}(1)<\gamma^{2}-1/4
occurs if and only if
\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,1)<1-(8\gamma^{2})^{-1}
(see (
106
)),
we can easily demonstrate that

\displaystyle\theta^{(t)}(1,a_{1})\leq\frac{1}{2}\log\left(8\gamma^{2}-1\right)\leq\frac{1}{2}\log 7\qquad\text{for all }t<t_{1}\big(\gamma^{2}-1/4\big).

(111)

If
t_{1}\big(\gamma^{2}-1/4\big)\geq\big\lceil\frac{32\log(7)c_{\mathrm{b},1}|\mathcal{S}|}{7\gamma^{3}c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta}\big\rceil
,
then taking
t=\big\lceil\frac{32\log(7)c_{\mathrm{b},1}|\mathcal{S}|}{7\gamma^{3}c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta}\big\rceil
together with (
110
) and (
12a
) yields

\theta^{(t)}(1,a_{1})\geq\theta^{(0)}(1,a_{1})+\eta\frac{7\gamma^{3}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{32c_{\mathrm{b},1}|\mathcal{S}|}t=\eta\frac{7\gamma^{3}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{32c_{\mathrm{b},1}|\mathcal{S}|}t\geq\log 7,

thus leading to contradiction with (
111
). As a result, one arrives at the following upper bound:

\displaystyle t_{1}({\tau}_{1})\leq t_{1}\big(\gamma^{2}-1/4\big)\leq\frac{32\log(7)c_{\mathrm{b},1}|\mathcal{S}|}{7\gamma^{3}c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta}\leq\frac{15c_{\mathrm{b},1}|\mathcal{S}|}{c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta},

(112)

with the proviso that
\gamma\geq 0.85
(so that
\tau_{1}\leq\gamma^{2}-1/4
).

An upper bound on
t_{2}(\gamma^{4}-1/4)
(and hence
t_{2}(\tau_{2})
) can be obtained in a completely analogous manner

t_{2}({\tau}_{2})\leq t_{2}\big(\gamma^{4}-1/4\big)\leq\frac{15c_{\mathrm{b},2}|\mathcal{S}|}{c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta},

provided that
\gamma\geq 0.95
(so that
\tau_{2}\leq\gamma^{4}-1/4
). We omit the proof of this part for the sake of brevity.

Step 5: develop a lower bound on
t_{2}({\tau}_{2})
.

Repeating the argument in (
106
) and (
111
),
we see that
V^{(t)}(2)\geq{\tau}_{2}
if and only if
\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,2)\geq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{{\tau}_{2}}{2\gamma^{4}}
, which is also equivalent to

\theta^{(t)}(2,a_{1})\geq\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{1}{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{{\tau}_{2}}{2\gamma^{4}}}-1\right)>\frac{1}{2}\log 3,

as long as
2{\tau}_{2}>\gamma^{4}
.
Of necessity, this implies that
\theta^{(t)}(2,a_{1})>\frac{1}{2}\log 3
when
t=t_{2}({\tau}_{2})
.
If
t_{2}({\tau}_{2})\leq\frac{|\mathcal{S}|\log 3}{2\eta\gamma^{4}\left(1+8c_{\mathrm{m}}/c_{\mathrm{b},2}\right)}
,
then invoking (
110
) and (
12a
) and taking
t=t_{2}({\tau}_{2})
yield

\theta^{(t)}(2,a_{1})\leq\theta^{(0)}(2,a_{1})+\eta\frac{\gamma^{4}}{2|\mathcal{S}|}\left(1+\frac{8c_{\mathrm{m}}}{c_{\mathrm{b},2}}\right)t=\frac{\eta\gamma^{4}t}{2|\mathcal{S}|}\left(1+\frac{8c_{\mathrm{m}}}{c_{\mathrm{b},2}}\right)\leq\frac{1}{2}\log 3,

thus resulting in contradiction. We can thus conclude that

\displaystyle t_{2}({\tau}_{2})>\frac{|\mathcal{S}|\log 3}{\eta\gamma^{4}\left(1+8c_{\mathrm{m}}/c_{\mathrm{b},2}\right)}>\frac{|\mathcal{S}|\log 3}{\eta(1+8c_{\mathrm{m}}/c_{\mathrm{b},2})}.

(113)

As an important byproduct, comparing (
113
) with (
112
) immediately reveals that

\displaystyle t_{2}({\tau}_{2})\geq t_{1}\big(\gamma^{2}-1/4\big)\geq t_{1}\big({\tau}_{1}),

(114)

with the proviso that
\frac{\log 3}{1+8c_{\mathrm{m}}/c_{\mathrm{b},2}}\geq\frac{15c_{\mathrm{b},1}}{c_{\mathrm{m}}}
and
\gamma\geq 0.87
(so that
\gamma^{2}-1/4>{\tau}_{1}
).

Step 6: develop a lower bound on
t_{1}({\tau}_{1})
.

Repeat the analysis in (
106
) and (
111
) to show that:
V^{(t)}(1)\geq{\tau}_{1}
if and only if

\theta^{(t)}(1,a_{1})\geq\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{1}{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{{\tau}_{1}}{2\gamma^{2}}}-1\right)>\frac{1}{2}\log 3.

Clearly, this lower bound should hold if
t=t_{1}({\tau}_{1})
.
In addition, in view of (
114
), one has
\min\{t_{1}({\tau}_{1}),t_{2}({\tau}_{2})\}=t_{1}({\tau}_{1})
.
If
t_{1}({\tau}_{1})\leq\frac{|\mathcal{S}|\log 3}{\eta\gamma^{2}(1+17c_{\mathrm{m}}/c_{\mathrm{b},1})}
,
then setting
t=t_{1}({\tau}_{1})=\min\{t_{1}({\tau}_{1}),t_{2}({\tau}_{2})\}
and applying (
110
) and (
12a
) lead to

\theta^{(t)}(1,a_{1})\leq\theta^{(0)}(1,a_{1})+\eta\frac{\gamma^{2}(1+17c_{\mathrm{m}}/c_{\mathrm{b},1})}{2|\mathcal{S}|}t=\frac{\eta\gamma^{2}t(1+17c_{\mathrm{m}}/c_{\mathrm{b},1})}{2|\mathcal{S}|}\leq\frac{1}{2}\log 3,

which is contradictory to the preceding lower bound. This in turn implies that

\displaystyle t_{1}(\tau_{1})\geq\frac{|\mathcal{S}|\log 3}{\eta\gamma^{2}(1+17c_{\mathrm{m}}/c_{\mathrm{b},1})}>\frac{|\mathcal{S}|\log 3}{\eta(1+17c_{\mathrm{m}}/c_{\mathrm{b},1})}.

(115)

C.2
Crossing times for the adjoint state
\overline{1}

We now move on to the proof of (
38b
).
Note that we have developed a lower bound on
t_{2}({\tau}_{2})
in (
113
).
In order to justify the advertised result
t_{2}({\tau}_{2})>t_{\overline{1}}\big(\gamma^{3}-1/4\big)
, it thus suffices to demonstrate that

\displaystyle t_{\overline{1}}\big(\gamma^{3}-1/4\big)\leq\frac{|\mathcal{S}|\log 3}{\eta(1+8c_{\mathrm{m}}/c_{\mathrm{b},2})},

(116)

a goal we aim to accomplish in this subsection.

To do so, we divide into two cases.
In the scenario where
t_{1}({\tau}_{1})\geq t_{\overline{1}}\big(\gamma^{3}-1/4\big)
,
the bound (
112
) derived previously immediately leads to the desired bound:

t_{\overline{1}}\big(\gamma^{3}-1/4\big)\leq t_{1}({\tau}_{1})\leq\frac{15c_{\mathrm{b},1}|\mathcal{S}|}{c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta}\leq\frac{|\mathcal{S}|\log 3}{\eta(1+8c_{\mathrm{m}}/c_{\mathrm{b},2})},

with the proviso that
\frac{15c_{\mathrm{b},1}}{c_{\mathrm{m}}}\leq\frac{\log 3}{1+8c_{\mathrm{m}}/c_{\mathrm{b},2}}
.
Consequently, the subsequent analysis concentrates on establishing (
116
) for the case where

t_{1}({\tau}_{1})<t_{\overline{1}}\big(\gamma^{3}-1/4\big).

In what follows, we divide into three stages and investigate each one separately, after presenting some basic gradient calculations that shall be invoked frequently.

Gradient characterizations.

To begin with, observe from (
12
) that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(\overline{1},a_{1})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d_{\mu}^{(t)}(\overline{1})\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}|\overline{1}\big)\Big(Q^{(t)}(\overline{1},a_{1})-V^{(t)}(\overline{1})\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d_{\mu}^{(t)}(\overline{1})\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)\Big(Q^{(t)}(\overline{1},a_{1})-\sum_{a\in\{a_{0},a_{1}\}}\pi^{(t)}\big(a\,|\,\overline{1}\big)Q^{(t)}(\overline{1},a)\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d_{\mu}^{(t)}(\overline{1})\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)\Big(Q^{(t)}(\overline{1},a_{1})-Q^{(t)}(\overline{1},a_{0})\Big),

(117a)

which makes use of the fact
\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)+\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)=1
.
Analogously, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(\overline{1},a_{0})}=-\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d_{\mu}^{(t)}(\overline{s})\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1})\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1})\Big(Q^{(t)}(\overline{1},a_{1})-Q^{(t)}(\overline{1},a_{0})\Big).

(117b)

Stage 1: any
t
obeying
t<t_{1}({\tau}_{1})
.

We start by looking at each term in the gradient expression (
117a
) separately.
First, note that when
t<t_{1}({\tau}_{1})
, one has
V^{(t)}(1)<{\tau}_{1}
,
which combined with (
61
) in Lemma
8
indicates that

Q^{(t)}(\overline{1},a_{1})=\gamma V^{(t)}(1)<\gamma\tau_{1}=Q^{(t)}(\overline{1},a_{0})
.
In fact, from the definition (
18a
) of
\tau_{1}
, the property (
61
) and Lemma
10
, we have

1/2\geq Q^{(t)}(\overline{1},a_{0})>Q^{(t)}(\overline{1},a_{1})=\gamma V^{(t)}(1)\geq 0.

Additionally, recall that
t_{1}({\tau}_{1})<t_{2}({\tau}_{2})
(see (
114
)).
Lemma
3
then tells us that
d^{(t)}_{\mu}(\overline{1})\leq 14c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)^{2}
during this stage.
Substituting these into (
117a
) and using
\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1})\leq 1
, we arrive at

\displaystyle 0\geq\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(\overline{1},a_{1})}\geq-7c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1}\big),

(118)

which together with the PG update rule (
12
) also indicates that
\theta^{(t)}(\overline{1},a_{1})
(and hence
\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)
)
is monotonically non-increasing with
t
in this stage.
Invoke the auxiliary fact in Lemma
14
to reach

\displaystyle\pi^{(t+1)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)-\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)\geq 2\eta\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)\frac{\partial{V}^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(\overline{1},a_{1})}\geq-14\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)\Big[\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)\Big]^{2}.

Taking the preceding recursive relation together with Lemma
11
and recalling the initialization
\pi^{(0)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)=1/2
,
we can guarantee that

\displaystyle\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)\geq\frac{1}{28\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)t+2}\qquad\text{for all }t\leq t_{1}({\tau}_{1})

(119)

provided that
14\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)\leq 1
.
In conclusion, the above calculation precludes
\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)
from decaying to zero too quickly,
an observation that is particularly useful for our analysis in Stage 3.

Stage 2: any
t
obeying
t_{1}({\tau}_{1})\leq t<t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)
.

The only step lies in extending the lower bound (
119
) to this stage.
From the definition (
31
) of
t_{1}({\tau}_{1})
as well as the monotonicity of
V^{(t)}(1)
(see Lemma
9
), we know that

V^{(t)}(1)\geq V^{(t_{1}({\tau}_{1}))}(1)\geq{\tau}_{1}\qquad\text{for all }t\geq t_{1}({\tau}_{1}),

provided that
\eta<(1-\gamma)^{2}/5
. This taken together with the property (
61
) in Lemma
8
reveals that

Q^{(t)}(\overline{1},a_{1})-Q^{(t)}(\overline{1},a_{0})\geq 0\qquad\text{for all }t\geq t_{1}({\tau}_{1}),

and hence
\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)
is non-decreasing in
t
during this stage.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)\geq\pi^{(t_{1}(\tau_{1}))}\big(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)\geq\frac{1}{28\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)t_{1}(\tau_{1})+2},\qquad t\geq t_{1}(\tau_{1}),

(120)

where the first inequality follows from the non-decreasing property established above, and the second inequality follows from the lower bound (
119
). In fact, we have established a lower bound on
\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)
that holds for the entire trajectory of the algorithm.

Stage 3: any
t
obeying
t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)\leq t\leq t_{\overline{1}}(\gamma^{3}-1/4)
.

To facilitate analysis, we single out a time threshold
t^{\prime}
as follows:

\displaystyle t^{\prime}\coloneqq\min\left\{t\,|\,\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1})<1/2\right\}.

(121)

We begin by developing an upper bound on
\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)
for any
t\geq\max\{t^{\prime},t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)\}
.
Towards this, with the help of (
61
) in Lemma
8
we make the observation that: for any
t\geq t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)
, one has

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(\overline{1},a_{1})-Q^{(t)}(\overline{1},a_{0})=\gamma V^{(t)}(1)-\gamma\tau_{1}\geq\gamma\big(\gamma^{2}-1/4\big)-\gamma\tau_{1}\geq 0.1

(122)

as long as
\gamma\geq 0.92
, which combined with (
117b
) indicates that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(\overline{1},a_{0})}<0\qquad\text{for all }t\geq t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4).

(123)

Recognizing that
d^{\pi}_{\mu}(\overline{1})\geq c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)^{2}
(see Lemma
2
),
we can continue the derivation (
117b
) to derive

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(\overline{1},a_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\gamma c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{2}\cdot\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1})\cdot 0.1=-0.05c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1})

for any
t\geq\max\{t^{\prime},t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)\}
, which implies

\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1})\geq\pi^{(t^{\prime})}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1})=1-\pi^{(t^{\prime})}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1})\geq 1/2\qquad\text{for any }t\geq t^{\prime}.

Invoke Lemma
14
to arrive at

\pi^{(t+1)}\big(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)-\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)\leq\frac{\eta}{2}\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)\frac{\partial{V}^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(\overline{1},a_{0})}\leq-\frac{\eta}{40}c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)\Big[\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1})\Big]^{2},

provided that
2\eta\frac{\partial{V}^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(\overline{1},a_{0})}\geq-1
, which is guaranteed by
\eta<(1-\gamma)/2
.
Recalling that
\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)\leq 1/2
for this entire stage,
one can apply Lemma
11
to obtain

\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)\leq\frac{1}{\frac{\eta}{40}c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)\Big(t-\max\big\{t^{\prime},t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)\big\}\Big)+2}

(124)

for any
t\geq\max\{t^{\prime},t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)\}
.

With the above upper bound (
124
) in place, we are capable of showing that the target quantity
t_{\overline{1}}\big(\gamma^{3}-1/4\big)
is not much larger than
\max\{t^{\prime},t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)\}
.
To show this, we first note that the value function of the adjoint state
\overline{1}
obeys (see Part (iii) in Lemma
8
)

\displaystyle V^{(t)}(\overline{1})

\displaystyle=\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1})Q^{(t)}(\overline{1},a_{0})+\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1})Q^{(t)}(\overline{1},a_{1})=\gamma\tau_{1}\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1})+\gamma\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1})V^{(t)}(1)

\displaystyle=\gamma\tau_{1}\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1})+\gamma V^{(t)}(1)\Big\{1-\pi(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1})\Big\}\geq\gamma\tau_{1}\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1})+\gamma\left(\gamma^{2}-1/4\right)\Big\{1-\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1})\Big\}

\displaystyle=\gamma\left\{\tau_{1}-\gamma^{2}+1/4\right\}\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1})+\gamma^{3}-\gamma/4,

where the inequality holds since
V^{(t)}(1)\geq\gamma^{2}-1/4
in this stage (given that
t\geq t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)
).
Recognizing that
0.5\gamma^{2/3}-\gamma^{2}+1/4<0
for any
\gamma\geq 0.85
, we can rearrange terms to demonstrate that
V^{(t)}(\overline{1})\geq\gamma^{3}-1/4
holds once

\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1})\leq\frac{1-\gamma}{4\gamma\left(\gamma^{2}-1/4-0.5\gamma^{2/3}\right)}.

In fact, for any
\gamma\geq 0.85
, the above inequality is guaranteed to hold as long as
\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1})\leq 1-\gamma
since
4\gamma\left(\gamma^{2}-1/4-0.5\gamma^{2/3}\right)<1
.
In view of (
124
), we can achieve
\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1})\leq 1-\gamma
as soon as

t-\max\left\{t^{\prime},t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)\right\}
surpasses
\frac{40}{c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}
.
As a consequence, we reach

\displaystyle t_{\overline{1}}\big(\gamma^{3}-1/4\big)\leq\max\left\{t^{\prime},t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)\right\}+\frac{40}{c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

(125)

Armed with the relation (
125
),
the goal of upper bounding
t_{\overline{1}}\big(\gamma^{3}-1/4\big)
can be accomplished by
controlling
t^{\prime}
. To this end, we claim for the moment that

\displaystyle t^{\prime}\leq\frac{1121t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)}{\gamma}.

(126)

If this claim holds, then combining it with (
125
) and (
112
) would result in the advertised bound (
116
):

\displaystyle t_{\overline{1}}\big(\gamma^{3}-1/4\big)\leq\frac{9972c_{\mathrm{b},1}|\mathcal{S}|}{\gamma^{4}c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta}+\frac{40}{c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}\leq\frac{|\mathcal{S}|}{4\gamma^{4}\eta}\leq\frac{|\mathcal{S}|\log 3}{\eta(1+8c_{\mathrm{m}}/c_{\mathrm{b},2})},

where the penultimate inequality relies on the assumptions
\frac{c_{\mathrm{b},1}}{c_{\mathrm{m}}}\leq\frac{1}{79776}
and
|\mathcal{S}|\geq\frac{320\gamma^{3}}{c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)^{2}}
,
and the last one holds as long as
\frac{1}{4\gamma^{4}}\leq\frac{\log 3}{1+8c_{\mathrm{m}}/c_{\mathrm{b},2}}
.
To finish up, it suffices to establish the claim (
126
).

Proof of the claim (
126
).

It is sufficient to consider the case where
t^{\prime}>t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)
;
otherwise the inequality (
126
) is trivially satisfied.
Since Lemma
2
tells us that
d^{\pi}_{\mu}(\overline{1})\geq c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)^{2}
, we can see from (
117a
) that, for any
t
with
t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)\leq t<t^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(\overline{1},a_{1})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d_{\mu}^{(t)}(\overline{1})\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1})\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1})\Big\{Q^{(t)}(\overline{1},a_{1})-Q^{(t)}(\overline{1},a_{0})\Big\}

\displaystyle\geq 0.05c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1})>0,

where the last line follows by combining (
122
) and the fact that
\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{1})\geq 1/2
for any
t<t^{\prime}
(see the definition (
121
) of
t^{\prime}
).
According to Lemma
14
, we can demonstrate that

\displaystyle\pi^{(t+1)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)-\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)

\displaystyle\geq\eta\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)\frac{\partial{V}^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(\overline{1},a_{1})}\geq 0.05\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)\Big[\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)\Big]^{2}

for any
t
obeying
t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)\leq t<t^{\prime}
. Invoking Lemma
11
, we then have

\displaystyle t^{\prime}\leq\frac{1+0.025\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)}{0.05\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4))}\big(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)}+t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)

\displaystyle<\frac{40}{\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4))}\big(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{1}\big)}+t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{40\big(28t_{1}(\tau_{1})+2\big)}{\gamma}+t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1121t_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1/4)}{\gamma}

as claimed, where the second line follows from (
120
).

C.3
Auxiliary facts

In this subsection, we collect some elementary facts that have been used multiple times in the proof of Lemma
4
.
Specifically, the lemma below makes clear an explicit link between the gradient
\nabla_{\theta}V^{(t)}(\mu)
and the difference between two consecutive policy iterates.

Lemma 14
.

Consider any
s
whose associated action space is
\{a_{0},a_{1}\}
.

•

If
\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\leq 0
, then one has

\displaystyle\pi^{(t+1)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,s\big)-\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,s\big)\geq 2\eta\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,s\big)\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}.

(127)

•

If
\pi^{(t+1)}\big(a_{0}\,|\,s\big)\geq 1/2
and
-1\leq 2\eta\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\leq 0
, then we have

\displaystyle\pi^{(t+1)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,s\big)-\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,s\big)\leq\frac{\eta}{2}\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,s\big)\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}.

(128)

•

If
\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\geq 0
and if
\pi^{(t+1)}\big(a_{0}\,|\,s\big)\geq 1/2
, then one has

\displaystyle\pi^{(t+1)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,s\big)-\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,s\big)\geq\eta\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,s\big)\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}.

(129)

Proof of Lemma
14
.

We make note of the following elementary identity

\displaystyle\frac{e^{\theta_{1}}}{e^{\theta_{1}}+e^{-\theta_{1}}}-\frac{e^{\theta_{2}}}{e^{\theta_{2}}+e^{-\theta_{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}}-e^{-\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}}}{\big(e^{\theta_{1}}+e^{-\theta_{1}}\big)\big(e^{\theta_{2}}+e^{-\theta_{2}}\big)}=\frac{e^{-\theta_{1}}}{e^{\theta_{1}}+e^{-\theta_{1}}}\frac{e^{\theta_{2}}}{e^{\theta_{2}}+e^{-\theta_{2}}}\left(e^{2(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})}-1\right)

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{e^{\theta_{1}}}{e^{\theta_{1}}+e^{-\theta_{1}}}\right)\frac{e^{\theta_{2}}}{e^{\theta_{2}}+e^{-\theta_{2}}}\left(e^{2(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})}-1\right),

which allows us to write

\displaystyle\pi^{(t+1)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,s\big)-\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,s\big)

\displaystyle=\pi^{(t+1)}\big(a_{0}\,|\,s\big)\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,s\big)\left\{\exp\Big[2\theta_{t+1}(s,a_{1})-2\theta_{t}(s,a_{1})\Big]-1\right\}

\displaystyle=\pi^{(t+1)}\big(a_{0}\,|\,s\big)\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,s\big)\left\{\exp\Big[2\eta\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\Big]-1\right\}.

(130)

•

If
\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\leq 0
, then one can deduce that

(
130
)

\displaystyle\geq 2\eta\pi^{(t+1)}\big(a_{0}\,|\,s\big)\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,s\big)\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\geq 2\eta\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,s\big)\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})},

where the first inequality relies on the elementary fact
e^{x}-1\geq x
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
, and the second one holds since
\pi^{(t+1)}\big(a_{0}\,|\,s\big)\leq 1
and
\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\leq 0
.

•

If
-1\leq 2\eta\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\leq 0
and
\pi^{(t+1)}\big(a_{0}\,|\,s\big)\geq 1/2
, then one has

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:pit-pi-tminus1-a1-diff}\leq\eta\pi^{(t+1)}\big(a_{0}\,|\,s\big)\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,s\big)\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\leq\frac{\eta}{2}\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,s\big)\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})},

where the first inequality comes from the elementary inequality
e^{x}-1\leq 0.5x
for any
-1\leq x\leq 0
,
and the last inequality is valid since
\pi^{(t+1)}\big(a_{0}\,|\,s\big)\geq 1/2
and
\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\leq 0
.

•

If
\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\geq 0
and if
\pi^{(t+1)}\big(a_{0}\,|\,s\big)\geq 1/2
, then it follows that

(
130
)

\displaystyle\geq 2\eta\pi^{(t+1)}\big(a_{0}\,|\,s\big)\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,s\big)\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\geq\eta\pi^{(t)}\big(a_{1}\,|\,s\big)\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})},

as claimed in (
129
).

∎

Appendix D
Analysis for the initial stage (Lemma
5
)

This section establishes Lemma
5
, which investigates the dynamics of
\theta^{(t)}(s,a)
prior to the threshold
t_{s-2}({\tau}_{s-2})
.
Before proceeding, let us introduce a rescaled version of
\pi^{(t)}(s,a)
that is sometimes convenient to work with:

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(s,a)\coloneqq\exp\Big(\theta^{(t)}(s,a)-\max_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}\theta^{(t)}(s,a^{\prime})\Big)

(131)

for any state-action pair
(s,a)
.
This is orderwise equivalent to
\pi^{(t)}(s,a)
since

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(s,a)

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp\big(\theta^{(t)}(s,a)\big)}{\max_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}\exp\left(\theta^{(t)}(s,a^{\prime})\right)}\geq\frac{\exp\big(\theta^{(t)}(s,a)\big)}{\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}\exp\left(\theta^{(t)}(s,a^{\prime})\right)}=\pi^{(t)}(s,a);

(132a)

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(s,a)

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp\big(\theta^{(t)}(s,a)\big)}{\max_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}\exp\left(\theta^{(t)}(s,a^{\prime})\right)}\leq\frac{\exp\big(\theta^{(t)}(s,a)\big)}{\frac{1}{3}\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}\exp\left(\theta^{(t)}(s,a^{\prime})\right)}=3\pi^{(t)}(s,a).

(132b)

D.1
Two key properties

Our proof is based on the following claim: in order to establish the advertised results of Lemma
5
,
it suffices to justify the following two properties

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+56c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta(1-\gamma)t}\leq\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\leq\frac{1}{1+\frac{c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma}{35}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}t}

(133)

\displaystyle\qquad\text{and}\qquad Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-V^{(t)}(s)\geq 0

(134)

hold for any
t\leq t_{s-2}({\tau}_{s-2})
.
In light of this claim, our subsequent analysis consists of validating these two inequalities separately,
which forms the main content of Section
D.2
.

We now move on to justify the above claim, namely, Lemma
5
is valid as long as the two key properties (
133
) and (
134
) hold true.
First, recall that Lemma
12
together with (
33
) and Lemma
4
tells us that

\displaystyle\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0})\geq\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\geq\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\qquad\text{for all }t<t_{s-2}({\tau}_{s-2})\leq t_{s-1}({\tau}_{s-1})

(135)

for any
3\leq s\leq H
.
Next, note that the gradient takes the following form (cf. (
12
))

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a)}=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d^{(t)}_{\mu}(s)\pi^{(t)}(a|s)\big(Q^{(t)}(s,a)-V^{(t)}(s)\big),\qquad a\in\{a_{0},a_{1},a_{2}\}

(136)

which together with the assumption
{Q}^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-{V}^{(t)}(s)\geq 0
(cf. (
134
)) implies that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{V}^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{2})}\geq 0\qquad\text{for all }t<t_{s-2}({\tau}_{s-2}).

Consequently,
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})
keeps increasing before
t
exceeds
t_{s-2}({\tau}_{s-2})
.
This combined with the relation (
135
), the initialization
\theta^{(0)}(s,a_{0})=\theta^{(0)}(s,a_{2})=0
and the constraint
\sum_{a}\theta^{(t)}(s,a)=0
(see Part (vii) of Lemma
8
) reveals that

\displaystyle\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0})\geq\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\geq 0\geq\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\qquad\text{for all }t<t_{s-2}(\tau_{s-2}),

(137)

thereby confirming the desired property (
41
).

Further, given the non-negativity of
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})
stated in (
137
), one can readily derive

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)

\displaystyle=\exp\Big(\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\max_{a^{\prime}}\theta^{(t)}(s,a^{\prime})\Big)=\exp\Big(\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0})\Big)

\displaystyle=\exp\Big(2\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})+\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\Big)\geq\exp\Big(2\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\Big),

where the last line also makes use of the identity
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0})=-\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})
(see Part (vii) of Lemma
8
).
With this observation in mind, the assumed property (
133
) directly leads to the advertised result (
40
).

D.2
Proof of the properties (
133
) and (
134
)

This subsection presents the proofs of the two key properties, which are somewhat intertwined and require a bit of induction.
Before proceeding, we make note of the initialization
\widehat{\pi}^{(0)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)=1
, which clearly satisfies the property (
133
) for this base case. Our proof consists of two steps to be detailed below. As can be easily seen, combining these two steps in an inductive manner immediately establishes both properties (
133
) and (
134
) for any
t\leq t_{s-2}({\tau}_{s-2})
.

Step 1: justifying (
134
) for the
t
-th iteration if (
133
) holds for the
t
-th iteration

We first turn to the proof of the inequality (
134
), assuming that (
133
) holds for the
t
-th iteration.
According to (
132
) and (
133
), we have

\displaystyle\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\geq\frac{1}{3}\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\geq\frac{1}{3+168c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta(1-\gamma)t}.

(138)

By virtue of the auxiliary fact (
146c
) in Lemma
15
(see Section
D.3
), one has

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\leq\frac{\gamma p}{\frac{c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma}{2}\eta(1-\gamma)t+\frac{1}{\gamma\tau_{s-2}}}.

(139)

Given that
p\coloneqq c_{\mathrm{p}}(1-\gamma)
for some small constant
0<c_{\mathrm{p}}<\frac{1}{2016}
, the above two inequalities allow one to ensure that

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})<(\gamma^{3/2}-\gamma^{2})\tau_{s-1}\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s).

(140)

With the above relation in mind, we are ready to control
Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-V^{(t)}(s)
as follows

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-V^{(t)}(s)

\displaystyle=\Big(\sum_{a}\pi^{(t)}(a\,|\,s)\Big)Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-\sum_{a}\pi^{(t)}(a\,|\,s)Q^{(t)}(s,a)

\displaystyle=\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\Big)-\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)(\gamma^{3/2}-\gamma^{2})\tau_{s-1}-\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\Big)

\displaystyle>0.

Here, the second lines arise from the auxiliary facts in Lemma
15
, while the last inequality is a consequence of (
140
).
Then we complete the proof of the inequality (
134
).

Step 2: justifying (
133
) for the
(t+1)
-th iteration if (
134
) holds up to the
t
-th iteration

Suppose that the inequality (
134
) holds up to the
t
-th iteration.
To validate (
133
) for the
(t+1)
-th iteration,
we claim for the moment that

\displaystyle-14c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\leq\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\leq-\frac{c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma}{24}(1-\gamma)^{2}\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)<0

(141)

as long as
t\leq t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
.
Let us take this claim as given, and return to prove it shortly.

Recall from (
135
) that

\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0})\geq\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\geq\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})

and hence

\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)=\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0})
is increasing with
t
during this stage, and as a result,

\theta^{(t+1)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})+\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)-\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t+1)}(s)\leq\theta^{(t+1)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\leq 0.

The gradient expression (
136
) combined with the satisfaction of (
134
) up to the
t
-th iteration implies that

\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})
is increasing up to the
t
-th iteration.
Given that
\sum_{a}\theta^{(t)}(s,a)=0
(see Part (vii) of Lemma
8
), we can derive

\displaystyle\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)-\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t+1)}(s)

\displaystyle=\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0})-\theta^{(t+1)}(s,a_{0})=\theta^{(t+1)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})+\theta^{(t+1)}(s,a_{2})-\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})

\displaystyle\geq\theta^{(t+1)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1}),

thus indicating that

\theta^{(t+1)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})+\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)-\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t+1)}(s)\geq 2\Big(\theta^{(t+1)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\Big).

These combined with Lemma
16
in Section
D.3
guarantee that

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(t+1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)-\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)

\displaystyle\geq 2\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\left(\theta^{(t+1)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\right),

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(t+1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)-\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)

\displaystyle\leq 0.7\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\left(\theta^{(t+1)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\right),

and as a consequence,

\displaystyle 2\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\cdot\eta\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{\pi}^{(t+1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)-\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\leq 0.7\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\cdot\eta\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}.

Taking this collectively with (
141
), we reach
