\displaystyle-28c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta(1-\gamma)\Big[\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\Big]^{2}\leq\widehat{\pi}^{(t+1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)-\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\leq-\frac{c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma}{35}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}\Big[\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\Big]^{2}.

(142)

Apply Lemma
11
together with the initialization
\widehat{\pi}^{(0)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)=1
to arrive at

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+56c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta(1-\gamma)(t+1)}\leq\widehat{\pi}^{(t+1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\leq\frac{1}{1+\frac{c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma}{35}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}(t+1)}.

(143)

Proof of the inequality (
141
).

Recall the gradient expression (
136
):

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d^{(t)}_{\mu}(s)\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-V^{(t)}(s)\Big),

(144)

each term of which will be bounded separately.

The first step is to control
Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-V^{(t)}(s)
, towards which we start with the following decomposition

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-V^{(t)}(s)=Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\sum\nolimits_{a\in\{a_{0},a_{1},a_{2}\}}\pi^{(t)}(a\,|\,s)Q^{(t)}(s,a)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=-\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\Big)-\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\Big).

(145)

The auxiliary facts stated in Lemma
15
(see Appendix
D.3
) imply that

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\geq Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\geq(\gamma^{3/2}-\gamma^{2})\tau_{s-1},

while Lemma
1
and Lemma
10
tell us that

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\leq V^{\star}(s)-0=\gamma^{2s}.

At the same time, the auxiliary fact (
146a
) in Lemma
15
(see Appendix
D.3
) taken together with the gradient expression (
12b
) guarantees that

\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{0})}\geq\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{2})}\geq\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}

and hence
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\leq\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\leq\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0})

(or equivalently
\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\leq\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\leq\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)
) during this stage. As a result,

\displaystyle\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\leq 1/3\qquad\text{and}\qquad 1\geq\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)+\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\geq 2/3.

Substituting the preceding bounds into the decomposition (
145
), we arrive at

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-V^{(t)}(s)

\displaystyle\leq-\left(\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)+\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\right)\min\left\{Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1}),Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{2}{3}(\gamma^{\frac{3}{2}}-\gamma^{2})\tau_{s-1}=-\frac{2}{3}\frac{\gamma^{\frac{3}{2}}\tau_{s-1}}{1+\sqrt{\gamma}}(1-\gamma)\leq-\frac{1-\gamma}{8},

provided that
\gamma\geq 0.85
. Meanwhile, it follows from Lemma
1
and Lemma
10
that

Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-V^{(t)}(s)\geq 0-V^{\star}(s)\geq-1.

Further, from Lemma
3
, we have learned that
c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)^{2}\leq d^{(t)}_{\mu}(s)\leq 14c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)^{2}
for any
t\leq t_{s}(\tau_{s})
.
Substituting the above bounds into (
144
) and invoking (
132
),
we establish the desired inequality (
141
).

D.3
Auxiliary facts

We now gather a few basic facts that are useful throughout this section.
The first lemma presents some preliminary facts regarding the difference of Q-function estimates across different actions in the current setting; the proof is deferred to Appendix
D.3.1
.

Lemma 15
.

Consider any
t<t_{s-2}({\tau}_{s-2})
.
Under the assumption (
35
), the following are satisfied

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})>Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})

\displaystyle>Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1}),

(146a)

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})

\displaystyle\geq(\gamma^{3/2}-\gamma^{2})\tau_{s-1},

(146b)

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\gamma p}{\frac{c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma}{2}\eta(1-\gamma)t+\frac{1}{\gamma\tau_{s-2}}}.

(146c)

Remark
10
.

Lemma
15
makes clear that — before
t
exceeds
t_{s-2}({\tau}_{s-2})
— action
a_{0}
is perceived as the best choice, with
a_{1}
being the least favorable one. In the meantime, it also reveals that (i)
Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})
is considerably larger than
Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})
, while
(ii) the gap between
Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})
and
Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})
decays at least as rapidly as
O(1/t)
in this stage.

The second lemma is concerned with the consecutive difference between two rescaled policy iterates.
The proof can be found in Appendix
D.3.2
.

Lemma 16
.

Suppose that
0<\eta\leq(1-\gamma)/6
.
For any
t\geq 0
and any
3\leq s\leq H
, define
\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)\coloneqq\max_{a}\theta^{(t)}(s,a)
.
If we write

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(t+1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)-\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)

\displaystyle=c\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\Big(\theta^{(t+1)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})+\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)-\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t+1)}(s)\Big)

(147)

for some
c\in\mathbb{R}
, then we necessarily have

\displaystyle c\in[1,1.5)\qquad

\displaystyle\text{if }\text{$\theta$}^{(t+1)}(s,a_{1})\geq\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})~\text{ and }~\text{$\theta$}_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)\geq\text{$\theta$}_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t+1)}(s);

\displaystyle c\in(0.72,1]\qquad

\displaystyle\text{if }\text{$\theta$}^{(t+1)}(s,a_{1})\leq\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})~\text{ and }~\text{$\theta$}_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)\leq\text{$\theta$}_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t+1)}(s).

D.3.1
Proof of Lemma
15

In view of Lemma
10
, one has
V^{(t)}(\overline{s-2})\geq 0
for all
t\geq 0
.
Therefore, the relation (
59
) yields

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})=r_{s}+\gamma pV^{(t)}(\overline{s-2})\geq r_{s}=\gamma^{3/2}\tau_{s-1}.

In addition, for any
t<t_{s-2}({\tau}_{s-2})\leq t_{s-1}({\tau}_{s-1})\leq t_{\overline{s-1}}(\gamma\tau_{s-1})
(see Lemma
8
and Lemma
4
),
we have
V^{(t)}(\overline{s-1})<\gamma\tau_{s-1}
, and hence it is seen from the relation (
59
) that

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})=Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-\gamma V^{(t)}(\overline{s-1})\geq(\gamma^{3/2}-\gamma^{2})\tau_{s-1}>0,

as claimed in (
146b
). Also, Part (i) of Lemma
8
tells us that

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})

\displaystyle=r_{s}+\gamma^{2}p\tau_{s-2}-r_{s}-\gamma pV^{(t)}(\overline{s-2})=\gamma p\Big(\gamma\tau_{s-2}-V^{(t)}(\overline{s-2})\Big)\geq 0,

where the last inequality holds for any
t<t_{s-2}({\tau}_{s-2})
(see Part (iii) of Lemma
8
).
These taken together validate (
146a
).

It remains to justify (
146c
), which is the content of the rest of this proof.
The main step lies in demonstrating that, for any
t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
and any
1\leq s\leq H
,

\displaystyle\gamma\tau_{s}-V^{(t)}(\overline{s})\leq\frac{1}{\frac{c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma}{2}\eta(1-\gamma)t+\frac{1}{\gamma\tau_{s}}}.

(148)

If this were true, than taking it together with the following property (which is a consequence of (
59
))

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})=\gamma p\left(\gamma\tau_{s-2}-V^{(t)}(\overline{s-2})\right),

(149)

would establish the inequality (
146c
).
It then boils down to justifying (
148
). Towards this, we first make the observation that

\displaystyle V^{(t)}(\overline{s})-\gamma\tau_{s}

\displaystyle=\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{0})+\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})-\gamma\tau_{s}

\displaystyle=\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})\gamma\tau_{s}+\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})-\gamma\tau_{s}

\displaystyle=\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\Big(Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})-\gamma\tau_{s}\Big),

(150)

where the second line holds since
Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{0})=\gamma\tau_{s}
(see (
61
)).
Additionally, recall from the definition that for any
t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
, one has
V^{(t)}(s)<{\tau}_{s}
and hence

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(\overline{s},a_{1})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d_{\mu}^{(t)}(\overline{s})\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\Big(Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})-\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{0})-\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d_{\mu}^{(t)}(\overline{s})\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\Big(Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})-Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{0})\Big)

(151)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d_{\mu}^{(t)}(\overline{s})\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\Big(\gamma V^{(t)}(s)-\gamma\tau_{s}\Big)<0,

where the last line makes use of the identities in (
61
).
This means that
\theta^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})
keeps decreasing, and hence
\theta^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})\leq 0
given the initialization
\theta^{(0)}(\overline{s},a_{1})=0
.
As an immediate consequence, one has
\theta^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{0})=-\theta^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})\geq 0
and
\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})\geq 1/2
.
Taking this observation together with (
151
) and Lemma
2
gives

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(\overline{s},a_{1})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d_{\mu}^{(t)}(\overline{s})\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\Big(Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})-Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{0})\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d_{\mu}^{(t)}(\overline{s})\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\Big(Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})-\gamma\tau_{s}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma}{2}(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\Big(Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})-\gamma\tau_{s}\Big)<0.

Moreover, combine (
151
) with Lemma
3
and Lemma
1
to yield

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(\overline{s},a_{1})}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d_{\mu}^{(t)}(\overline{s})\Big|Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})-\gamma\tau_{s}\Big|\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}14c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)^{2}\Big(\big|Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})\big|+\gamma\tau_{s}\Big)\leq 28c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma),

If
28c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta(1-\gamma)<1/2
, then the above inequalities taken together with Lemma
14
give

\displaystyle\pi^{(t+1)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})-\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma}{2}\eta(1-\gamma)\Big[\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\Big]^{2}\Big(Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})-\gamma\tau_{s}\Big)

(152)

for all
t<t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
.
This combined with (
150
) and the monotonicity of
Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})
(see Lemma
9
) gives

\displaystyle\gamma\tau_{s}-V^{(t+1)}(\overline{s})

\displaystyle=\pi^{(t+1)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\Big(\gamma\tau_{s}-Q^{(t+1)}(\overline{s},a_{1})\Big)\leq\pi^{(t+1)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\Big(\gamma\tau_{s}-Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\Big\{\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})-\frac{c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma}{2}\eta(1-\gamma)\Big[\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\Big]^{2}\Big(\gamma\tau_{s}-Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})\Big)\Big\}\Big(\gamma\tau_{s}-Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big\{1-\frac{\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)}{2}\Big(\gamma\tau_{s}-V^{(t)}(\overline{s})\Big)\Big\}\Big(\gamma\tau_{s}-V^{(t)}(\overline{s})\Big),

where the penultimate line follows from the inequality (
152
) for the iteration
t-1
,
and the last identity makes use of (
150
).
In conclusion, we have arrived at the following inductive relation

\displaystyle\gamma\tau_{s}-V^{(t+1)}(\overline{s})\leq\gamma\tau_{s}-V^{(t)}(\overline{s})-\frac{\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)}{2}\Big(\gamma\tau_{s}-V^{(t)}(\overline{s})\Big)^{2},

which bears resemblance to the recursive relations studied in Lemma
11
.
Recognizing that

\gamma\tau_{s}-V^{(0)}(\overline{s})\leq\gamma\tau_{s-2}
(since
V^{(0)}(\overline{s})\geq 0
according to Lemma
10
),
we can invoke Lemma
11
to derive

\gamma\tau_{s}-V^{(t)}(\overline{s})\leq\frac{1}{\frac{c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma}{2}\eta(1-\gamma)t+\frac{1}{\gamma\tau_{s}}}.

Putting the above pieces together concludes the proof of (
146c
).

D.3.2
Proof of Lemma
16

From the definition (
131
), direct calculations lead to

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(t+1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)-\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)

\displaystyle=\exp\Big(\theta^{(t+1)}(s,a_{1})-\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t+1)}(s)\Big)-\exp\Big(\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)\Big)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\Big\{\exp\Big(\theta^{(t+1)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})+\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)-\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t+1)}(s)\Big)-1\Big\}.

According to Lemma
1
, we have
|{Q}^{(t)}(s,a)|\leq 1
and
|{V}^{(t)}(s)|\leq 1
, which indicates — for any action
a\in\{a_{0},a_{1},a_{2}\}
— that

\Big|\theta^{(t+1)}(s,a)-\theta^{(t)}(s,a)\Big|=\Big|\eta\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a)}\Big|=\frac{\eta}{1-\gamma}d^{(t)}_{\mu}(s)\pi^{(t)}(a\,|\,s)\Big|{Q}^{(t)}(s,a)-{V}^{(t)}(s)\Big|\leq\frac{1}{3},

provided that
\eta\leq(1-\gamma)/6
. An immediate consequence is that

|\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t+1)}(s)-\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)|\leq 1/3
and hence

\Big|\theta^{(t+1)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})+\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)-\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t+1)}(s)\Big|\leq 2/3.

This taken together with the following elementary facts

\displaystyle(e^{x}-1)/x\,\in[1,1.5)\quad\text{for}\ 0\leq x<2/3,\qquad\text{and}\qquad(e^{x}-1)/x\,\in(0.72,1]\quad\text{for}\ -2/3<x\leq 0

establishes the claim (
147
).

Appendix E
Analysis for the intermediate stage (Lemma
6
)

We now turn attention to Lemma
6
, which studies the dynamics during an intermediate stage between
t_{s-2}({\tau}_{s-2})
and
t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})
.

E.1
Main steps

Key facts regarding crossing times.

Our proof for Lemma
6
relies on several crucial properties regarding the crossing times for both the key primary states and the adjoint states, as stated in the following two lemmas.

Lemma 17
.

Suppose that (
35
) holds.
There exists some constant
0<c_{0}\leq\frac{1222}{c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma}
such that:

\displaystyle t_{s}\big(\gamma^{2s}-1/4\big)-\max\Big\{t_{\overline{s-1}}\big(\gamma^{2s-1}-1/4\big),\,t_{s}({\tau}_{s})\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}

(153)

holds for every
3\leq s\leq H
, and

\displaystyle t_{\overline{s}}\big(\gamma^{2s+1}-1/4\big)-\max\Big\{t_{s}\big(\gamma^{2s}-1/4\big),\,t_{\overline{s}}(\tau_{s+1})\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}

(154)

holds for every
1\leq s\leq H
.

Lemma 18
.

Suppose that (
35
) holds and

\displaystyle t_{3}({\tau}_{3})>t_{2}(\gamma^{4}-1/4).

(155)

Then for every
3\leq s\leq H
, we have

\displaystyle t_{s}\big(\gamma^{2s}-1/4)-t_{s}({\tau}_{s}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2sc_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}},

(156a)

\displaystyle t_{\overline{s-1}}\big(\gamma^{2s-1}-1/4\big)-t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2sc_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

(156b)

In addition, if we further have
t_{s-1}({\tau}_{s-1})>t_{\overline{s-2}}(\tau_{s-1})+\frac{2sc_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}
, then

\displaystyle t_{\overline{s-2}}\big(\gamma^{2s-3}-1/4\big)

\displaystyle\leq t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}).

(156c)

Furthermore, (
156c
) still holds for
s=3
without requiring the assumption (
155
).

The proofs of the above two lemmas are postponed to Appendix
E.2
and Appendix
E.3
, respectively.
Let us take a moment to explain these two lemmas; to provide some intuitions, let us treat
\gamma^{2s}\approx 1
.
Lemma
17
makes clear that: once the value function estimates for states
\overline{s-1}
and
s
are both sufficiently large
(i.e.,
V^{(t)}(\overline{s-1})\gtrapprox 0.75
and
V^{(t)}(s)\gtrapprox 0.5
), then it does not take long for
V^{(t)}(s)
to (approximately) exceed
0.75
.
A similar message holds true if we replace
s
(resp.
\overline{s-1}
) with
\overline{s}
(resp.
s
).
Built upon this observation, Lemma
18
further reveals that: the time taken for
V^{(t)}(s)
(resp.
V^{(t)}(\overline{s-1})
)
to rise from
0.5
to
0.75
is fairly short.

Proof of Lemma
6
.

We are now in a position to present the proof of Lemma
6
.
To begin with, recall from Lemma
8
that: for any
t\leq t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})\leq t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s-1})
, one has

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})=\gamma V^{(t)}(\overline{s-1})\leq\gamma\tau_{s}\leq\min\Big\{Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0}),Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\Big\}.

(157)

Given that
V^{(t)}(s)
is a convex combination of
\{Q^{(t)}(s,a)\}_{a\in\{a_{0},a_{1},a_{2}\}}
,
one has
V^{(t)}(s)-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\geq 0
, which together with the gradient expression (
136
) indicates that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{V}^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\leq 0

(158)

and hence
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})
is non-increasing with
t
for any
t<t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})
.
Additionally, we have learned from Lemma
18
that

t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})\geq t_{\overline{s-2}}\big(\gamma^{2s-3}-1/4\big)\geq t_{\overline{s-2}}\big(\gamma{\tau}_{s-2}\big)=t_{s-2}\big({\tau}_{s-2}\big),

where the second inequality holds since
\gamma^{2s-3}-1/4\geq\gamma{\tau}_{s-2}
,
and the last identity results from Part (iii) of Lemma
8
.
This combined with the non-increasing nature of
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})
readily establishes the advertised inequality

\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})\leq\theta^{(t_{s-2}({\tau}_{s-2}))}(s,a_{1})
.

The next step is to justify
\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{2})\geq 0
.
Notice that for
t>t_{s-2}(\tau_{s-2})
, we have
V^{(t)}(s-2)>\tau_{s-2}
, and then
V^{(t)}(\overline{s-2})>\gamma\tau_{s-2}
by (
62
), which leads to
Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})>Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})
by (
59
) in Lemma
8
.
Recall (
157
) that
Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\leq\gamma\tau_{s}\leq\min\Big\{Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0}),Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\Big\}
.
Then, one has
Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-V^{(t)}(s)\geq 0
, which together with the gradient expression (
136
) indicates that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{V}^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{2})}\geq 0

(159)

and hence
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})
is non-decreasing with
t
for any
t<t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})
.
This establishes
\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{2})\geq 0
.

E.2
Proof of Lemma
17

For every
t\geq\max\big\{t_{\overline{s-1}}(\gamma^{2s-1}-1/4),t_{s}({\tau}_{s})\big\}
,
we isolate the following properties that will prove useful.

•

The definition (
30
) of
t_{\overline{s-1}}(\cdot)
together with the monotonicity property in Lemma
9
requires that

V^{(t)}(\overline{s-1})\geq\gamma^{2s-1}-1/4
,
and hence it is seen from (
59
) that

Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})=\gamma V^{(t)}(\overline{s-1})\geq\gamma^{2s}-\gamma/4.

(160)

•

In the meantime, since
t\geq t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
, Lemma
8
(cf. (
60
)) guarantees that

\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\geq(1-\gamma)/2.

(161)

•

Given that
t_{s}({\tau}_{s})\geq t_{s-2}(\tau_{s-2})
(see (
33
)) and the monotonicity property in Lemma
9
, one has
V^{(t)}(\overline{s-2})\geq\tau_{s-2}
, and thus we can see from (
59
) that

Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})=\gamma p\big(V^{(t)}(\overline{s-2})-\tau_{s-2}\big)\geq 0.

(162)

•

In addition, Lemma
8
ensures that both
Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})
and
Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})
are bounded above by
\gamma^{1/2}\tau_{s}
. Therefore, it is easily seen that

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\geq\gamma^{2s}-\gamma/4>\gamma^{1/2}\tau_{s}\geq Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\geq Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0}),

(163)

where the first inequality comes from (
160
), the second one holds when
\gamma^{2s}>0.75
, and the last inequality has been justified in (
162
).

•

Moreover, given that
V^{(t)}(s)\geq{\tau}_{s}
(since
t\geq t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
), one further has

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\max\big\{Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2}),Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})\big\}

\displaystyle>V^{(t)}(s)-\max\big\{Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2}),Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})\big\}

\displaystyle>{\tau}_{s}-\gamma^{1/2}\tau_{s}>0.

(164)

Here, the first inequality comes from (
163
), while the penultimate inequality is a consequence of (
163
).

•

We have seen from the above bullet points that

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})>V^{(t)}(s)>\max\big\{Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2}),Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})\big\},

(165)

which combined with the gradient expression (
136
) reveals that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}>0>\max\left\{\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{0})},\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{2})}\right\}.

(166)

With the above properties in place, we are now ready to prove our lemma, for which we shall look at the key primary states
3\leq s\leq H
and the adjoint states separately.

Analysis for the key primary states.

Let us start with any state
3\leq s\leq H
, and control
t_{s}(\gamma^{2s}-1/4)
as claimed in (
153
).
As before, define

\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)\coloneqq\max\nolimits_{a}\theta^{(t)}(s,a).

From the above fact (
166
), we know that
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})
keeps increasing with
t
while
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0}),\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})
are both decreasing with
t
. As a result, once
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})=\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)
, then
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})
will remain equal to
\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)
for the subsequent iterations. This allows us to divide into two stages as follows.

•

Stage 1: the duration when
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})<\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)
.

Our aim is to show that this stage contains at most
O\big(\frac{1}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}\big)
iterations.
In order to prove this, the starting point is again the gradient expression (
136
):

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d^{(t)}_{\mu}(s)\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-V^{(t)}(s)\big)

\displaystyle\geq c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-V^{(t)}(s)\big),

(167)

where the last line relies on Lemma
2
and the fact
Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})>V^{(t)}(s)
(cf. (
165
)).
Regarding the size of
Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-V^{(t)}(s)
, we make the observation that

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-V^{(t)}(s)

\displaystyle=\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})\Big)+\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\big(\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)+\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\big)\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\max_{a\in\{a_{0},a_{2}\}}Q^{(t)}(s,a)\Big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\mathrm{i})}}{{\geq}}\frac{1}{2}\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\max_{a\in\{a_{0},a_{2}\}}Q^{(t)}(s,a)\Big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\mathrm{ii})}}{{\geq}}\frac{1}{2}\big(\gamma^{2s}-\gamma/4-\gamma^{1/2}\tau_{s}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\mathrm{iii})}}{{\geq}}\frac{1}{16}.

Here, (i) follows since
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})<\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)
during this stage and, therefore,
\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\leq 1/2
;
(ii) arises from the relation (
163
); and (iii)
holds whenever
\gamma^{2s}-\gamma/4>5/8
.
Substitution into (
•
‣
E.2
) yields

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\geq\frac{1}{16}c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\geq\frac{1}{48}c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s),

(168)

where the last inequality comes from (
132
).
In addition, recall that
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})
is increasing with
t
, while
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0})
and
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})
are both decreasing (and hence
\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)
is also decreasing).
Invoking Lemma
16
then yields

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(t+1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)-\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)

\displaystyle\geq\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\Big(\text{$\theta$}^{(t+1)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})+\text{$\theta$}_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)-\text{$\theta$}_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t+1)}(s)\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\Big(\text{$\theta$}^{(t+1)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\Big)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\cdot\eta\frac{\partial{V}^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\geq\frac{1}{48}c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta\gamma(1-\gamma)\Big[\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\Big]^{2},

where the last line arises from (
168
).
Given this recursive relation, Lemma
11
implies that:
if
\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)<1
(or equivalently,
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})<\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)
), then one necessairly has

\displaystyle t-t_{0,1}\leq\frac{1+\frac{1}{48}c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta\gamma(1-\gamma)}{\frac{1}{48}c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta\gamma(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t_{0})}(a_{1}\,|\,s)}\leq\frac{2}{\frac{1}{48}c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta\gamma(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t_{0,1})}(a_{1}\,|\,s)}\leq\frac{240}{c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta\gamma(1-\gamma)^{2}},

with
t_{0,1}\coloneqq\max\big\{t_{\overline{s-1}}(\gamma^{2s-1}-1/4),t_{s}({\tau}_{s})\big\}
.
Here, the last inequality relies on the property (
161
).

•

Stage 2: the duration when
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})=\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)
.

For this stage, we intend to demonstrate that it takes at most
O\big(\frac{1}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}\big)
iterations to achieve
\max\big\{\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s),\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\big\}\leq(1-\gamma)/8
.
To this end, we again begin by studying the gradient as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{2})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d^{(t)}_{\mu}(s)\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-V^{(t)}(s)\Big)\leq c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-V^{(t)}(s)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{3}c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-V^{(t)}(s)\Big).

Here, the first inequality comes from Lemma
2
and the fact
Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})<V^{(t)}(s)
(see (
165
)),
whereas the last inequality is a consequence of (
132
).
In order to control
Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-V^{(t)}(s)
, we observe that

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-V^{(t)}(s)

\displaystyle=\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\Big)+\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\Big(\gamma^{1/2}\tau_{s}-\gamma^{2s}+\gamma/4\Big)+\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\gamma p\left(V^{(t)}(\overline{s-2})-\tau_{s-2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{3}\Big(\gamma^{1/2}\tau_{s}-\gamma^{2s}+\gamma/4\Big)+\gamma p\leq-\frac{1}{24},

where the second line arises from (
163
) and (
162
),
and the last line holds since
V^{(t)}(\overline{s-2})\leq 1
as well as the fact
\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\geq 1/3
during this stage (since
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})=\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)
). Putting the above two bounds together leads to

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{2})}\leq-\frac{1}{72}c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s).

(169)

Next, Lemma
16
tells us that

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(t+1)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)-\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)

\displaystyle\leq 0.72\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\Big(\text{$\theta$}^{(t+1)}(s,a_{2})-\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})+\text{$\theta$}_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)-\text{$\theta$}_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t+1)}(s)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 0.72\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\Big(\text{$\theta$}^{(t+1)}(s,a_{2})-\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\Big)=0.72\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\cdot\eta\frac{\partial{V}^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{2})}

\displaystyle\leq-0.01\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)\Big[\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\Big]^{2},

where the first inequality makes use of the facts
\theta^{(t+1)}(s,a_{2})\leq\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})

and
\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t)}(s)=\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\leq\theta^{(t+1)}(s,a_{1})=\theta_{\mathsf{max}}^{(t+1)}(s)
(see (
166
)).
Denoting by
t_{0,2}
the first iteration in this stage, we can invoke Lemma
11
to reach

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(t-t_{0,2})}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\leq\frac{1}{0.01\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)(t-t_{0,2})+1}.

(170)

As a consequence, once
t-t_{0,2}
exceeds

\frac{800}{\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)^{2}},

then one has
\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\leq(1-\gamma)/8
. The same conclusion holds for
a_{0}
as well.

Combining the above analysis for the two stages, we see that: if

t-t_{0,1}\geq\frac{240}{\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{800}{\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)^{2}}=\frac{1040}{\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)^{2}}

with
t_{0,1}\coloneqq\max\big\{t_{\overline{s-1}}(\gamma^{2s-1}-1/4),t_{s}({\tau}_{s})\big\}
, then one has

\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)=1-\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)-\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\geq 1-(1-\gamma)/4,

which combined with (
163
) leads to

\displaystyle V^{(t)}(s)\geq\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\geq\big(1-(1-\gamma)/4\big)\big(\gamma^{2s}-\gamma/4\big)\geq\gamma^{2s}-1/4.

This means that one necessairly has
t\geq t_{s}(\gamma^{2s}-1/4)
. It then follows that

\displaystyle t_{s}\big(\gamma^{2s}-1/4\big)-\max\big\{t_{\overline{s-1}}\big(\gamma^{2s-1}-1/4\big),t_{s}({\tau}_{s})\big\}=t_{s}\big(\gamma^{2s}-1/4\big)-t_{0,1}\leq\frac{1040}{\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)^{2}},

thus concluding the proof of (
153
).

Analysis for the adjoint states.

We then move forward to the adjoint states
\{\overline{1},\cdots,\overline{H}\}
and control
t_{\overline{s}}(\gamma^{2s+1}-1/4)
as desired in (
154
).
The proof consists of studying the dynamic for any
t
obeying

\max\Big\{t_{s}\big(\gamma^{2s}-1/4\big),\,t_{\overline{s}}(\tau_{s+1})\Big\}\leq t\leq t_{\overline{s}}\big(\gamma^{2s+1}-1/4\big).

Once again, we divide into two stages and analyze each of them separately.

•

Stage 1: the duration where
\theta^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})<\theta^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{0})
.

We aim to demonstrate that it takes no more than
O\big(\frac{1}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}\big)
iterations for
\theta^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})
to surpass
\theta^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{0})
.
In order to do so, note that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(\overline{s},a_{1})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d_{\mu}^{(t)}(\overline{s})\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})\Big(Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})-Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{0})\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{16}c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})>0.

(171)

Here, the last line applies Lemma
2
and makes use of the fact

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})-Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{0})=\gamma V^{(t)}(s)-\gamma\tau_{s}\geq\gamma(\gamma^{2s}-1/4-\tau_{s})\geq 1/8.

(172)

where the inequality comes from the assumption
t\geq t_{s}\big(\gamma^{2s}-1/4\big)
as well as the monotonicity property in Lemma
9
.
As a result, the PG update rule (
12a
) implies that
\theta^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})
is increasing in
t
,
and hence
\theta^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{0})
is decreasing in
t
(since
\sum_{a}\theta^{(t)}(s,a)=0
); these taken collectively mean that

\theta^{(t+1)}(\overline{s},a_{1})-\theta^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})+\theta^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{0})-\theta^{(t+1)}(\overline{s},a_{0})\geq\theta^{(t+1)}(\overline{s},a_{1})-\theta^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})\geq 0.

Invoking Lemma
16
then reveals that

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(t+1)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})-\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})

\displaystyle\geq\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\Big(\theta^{(t+1)}(\overline{s},a_{1})-\theta^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})+\theta^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{0})-\theta^{(t+1)}(\overline{s},a_{0})\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\Big(\theta^{(t+1)}(\overline{s},a_{1})-\theta^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})\Big)=\eta\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\frac{\partial{V}^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(\overline{s},a_{1})}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{48}\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)\Big[\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\Big]^{2},

where the last inequality relies on (
171
) and (
132
).
Given this recursive relation, Lemma
11
tells us that: one has
\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\geq 1
(which means
a_{1}
becomes the favored action by (
131
)) as soon as
t-t_{0,3}
exceeds

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\frac{1}{48}\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)\widehat{\pi}^{(t_{0,3})}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})}\leq\frac{96}{\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma){\pi}^{(t_{0,3})}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})}\leq\frac{1152}{\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)^{2}},

where
t_{0,3}\coloneqq\max\Big\{t_{s}\big(\gamma^{2s}-1/4\big),\,t_{\overline{s}}(\tau_{s+1})\Big\}
.
Here, the last inequality is valid as long as

\displaystyle\pi^{(t_{0,3})}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\geq(1-\gamma)/12

(173)

holds. It thus remains to justify (
173
). Towards this end, observe that for any
t\geq t_{\overline{s}}(\tau_{s+1})
,

\displaystyle\tau_{s+1}

\displaystyle\leq V^{(t)}(\overline{s})=\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{0})+\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})=\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})\gamma\tau_{s}+\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\gamma V^{(t)}(s)

\displaystyle=\gamma\tau_{s}+\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\gamma\left(V^{(t)}(s)-\tau_{s}\right)\leq\gamma\tau_{s}+\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\gamma,

and, as a result,

\displaystyle\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\tau_{s+1}}{\gamma}-\tau_{s}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\gamma^{\frac{2}{3}s+\frac{2}{3}}-\gamma^{\frac{2}{3}s+1}}{\gamma}=\frac{\gamma^{\frac{2}{3}s-1}}{2}\left(\gamma^{\frac{2}{3}}-\gamma\right)\geq\frac{1-\gamma}{12},

provided that
\gamma\geq 0.9
(so that
\gamma^{\frac{2}{3}}-\gamma\geq 0.3(1-\gamma)
)
and
\gamma^{\frac{2}{3}H}\geq 0.7
. This concludes the analysis of this stage.

•

Stage 2: the duration where
\theta^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})\geq\theta^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{0})
.

Similar to the above argument, we intend to show that it takes at most
O\big(\frac{1}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}\big)
iterations for

\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})\leq 1-\gamma
to occur.
From the gradient expression and the property (
172
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(\overline{s},a_{0})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d^{(t)}_{\mu}(\overline{s})\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\Big(Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{0})-Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{16}c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma){\pi}^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})\leq-\frac{1}{48}c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s}),

where the first inequality uses Lemma
2
and the property
\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\geq 1/2
(since
\theta^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})\geq\theta^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{0})
), and the last inequality relies on (
132
).
Repeating a similar argument as above, we can demonstrate that

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(t+1)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})-\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})\leq-\frac{1}{70}\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)\Big[\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})\Big]^{2}.

(174)

This combined with Lemma
11
implies that

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})\leq\frac{1}{\frac{1}{70}\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)(t-t_{0,4})+1},

(175)

with
t_{0,4}
denoting the first iteration of this stage.
Consequently, one has
\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})\leq 1-\gamma
— and therefore
\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})\leq 1-\gamma
according to (
132
) — as soon as
t-t_{0,4}
exceeds

\frac{70}{\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

Finally, if
\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})\leq 1-\gamma
, then one has

\displaystyle V^{(t)}(\overline{s})

\displaystyle=\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{0})+\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})=\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})\gamma\tau_{s}+\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,\overline{s})\gamma V^{(t)}(s)

\displaystyle\geq\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})\gamma\tau_{s}+\Big(1-\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})\Big)\gamma\left(\gamma^{2s}-1/4\right)=\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,\overline{s})\left(\gamma\tau_{s}-\gamma^{2s+1}-\gamma/4\right)+\gamma^{2s+1}-\gamma/4

\displaystyle\geq(1-\gamma)\left(\gamma\tau_{s}-\gamma^{2s+1}-\gamma/4\right)+\gamma^{2s+1}-\gamma/4\geq\gamma^{2s+1}-1/4,

where the first inequality holds by recalling that
t\geq t_{s}(\gamma^{2s}-1/4)
.
Consequently, putting the above pieces (regarding the duration of the two stages) together allows us to conclude that

t_{\overline{s}}\big(\gamma^{2s+1}-1/4\big)-\max\Big\{t_{s}\big(\gamma^{2s}-1/4\big),\,t_{\overline{s}}(\tau_{s+1})\Big\}\leq\frac{1152}{\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{70}{\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)^{2}}=\frac{1222}{\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)^{2}}

as claimed.

E.3
Proof of Lemma
18

Before proceeding, we first single out two properties that play a crucial role in the proof of Lemma
18
.

Lemma 19
.

The following basic properties hold true for any
2\leq s\leq H
:

\displaystyle t_{s}({\tau}_{s})

\displaystyle\geq t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s});

(176a)

\displaystyle t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})

\displaystyle\geq t_{s-1}({\tau}_{s-1}).

(176b)

The proof of this auxiliary lemma is deferred to the end of this subsection.
Equipped with this result, we are positioned to present the proof of Lemma
18
.
To begin with, we seek to bound the quantity
t_{s}(\gamma^{2s}-1/4)-t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
.
Apply Lemma
17
with a little algebra to yield

\displaystyle t_{s}(\gamma^{2s}-1/4)-t_{s}({\tau}_{s})

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big\{t_{\overline{s-1}}(\gamma^{2s-1}-1/4),t_{s}({\tau}_{s})\Big\}+\frac{c_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}-t_{s}({\tau}_{s})

\displaystyle=\max\Big\{t_{\overline{s-1}}(\gamma^{2s-1}-1/4)-t_{s}({\tau}_{s}),0\Big\}+\frac{c_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

(177)

With the assistance of the bound (
176a
) in Lemma
19
, we can
continue the bound in (
177
) to derive

\displaystyle t_{s}(\gamma^{2s}-1/4)-t_{s}({\tau}_{s})

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big\{t_{\overline{s-1}}(\gamma^{2s-1}-1/4)-t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}),0\Big\}+\frac{c_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}

\displaystyle=t_{\overline{s-1}}(\gamma^{2s-1}-1/4)-t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})+\frac{c_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

(178)

To continue, we shall bound the quantity
t_{\overline{s-1}}(\gamma^{2s-1}-1/4)-t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})
.
Similar to the derivation of the inequality (
177
), we can apply Lemma
17
to show that

\displaystyle t_{\overline{s-1}}(\gamma^{2s-1}-1/4)-t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big\{t_{s-1}(\gamma^{2s-2}-1/4),t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})\Big\}+\frac{c_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}-t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})

\displaystyle=\max\Big\{t_{s-1}(\gamma^{2s-2}-1/4)-t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}),0\Big\}+\frac{c_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big\{t_{s-1}(\gamma^{2s-2}-1/4)-t_{s-1}({\tau}_{s-1}),0\Big\}+\frac{c_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}

\displaystyle=t_{s-1}(\gamma^{2s-2}-1/4)-t_{s-1}({\tau}_{s-1})+\frac{c_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}},

(179)

where the third line makes use of (
176b
) in Lemma
19
.

Applying the inequalities (
178
) and (
179
) recursively, one arrives at

\displaystyle t_{s}(\gamma^{2s}-1/4)-t_{s}({\tau}_{s})

\displaystyle\leq t_{s-1}(\gamma^{2s-2}-1/4)-t_{s-1}({\tau}_{s-1})+\frac{2c_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}\leq\cdots

\displaystyle\leq t_{3}(\gamma^{6}-1/4)-t_{3}({\tau}_{3})+\frac{2(s-3)c_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

(180)

To continue, note that Lemma
17
and the bound (
176a
) give

\displaystyle t_{3}(\gamma^{6}-1/4)\leq

\displaystyle~\max\Big\{t_{\overline{2}}(\gamma^{5}-1/4),t_{3}(\tau_{3})\Big\}+\frac{c_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}},

\displaystyle t_{\overline{2}}(\gamma^{5}-1/4)\leq

\displaystyle~\max\Big\{t_{2}(\gamma^{4}-1/4),t_{\overline{2}}(\tau_{3})\Big\}+\frac{c_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}\leq\max\Big\{t_{2}(\gamma^{4}-1/4),t_{3}(\tau_{3})\Big\}+\frac{c_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}},

which together leads to

\displaystyle t_{3}(\gamma^{6}-1/4)\leq\max\Big\{t_{2}(\gamma^{4}-1/4),t_{3}(\tau_{3})\Big\}+\frac{2c_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

(181)

Plugging back to (
180
) leads to

\displaystyle t_{s}(\gamma^{2s}-1/4)-t_{s}({\tau}_{s})

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big\{t_{2}(\gamma^{4}-1/4),t_{3}({\tau}_{3})\Big\}-t_{3}({\tau}_{3})+\frac{2c_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{2(s-3)c_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(2s-4)c_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}},

(182)

where the last step arises from the assumption (
155
), that is,
t_{2}(\gamma^{4}-1/4)<t_{3}({\tau}_{3})
.

Further, the above inequality taken together with (
179
) yields

\displaystyle t_{\overline{s-1}}(\gamma^{2s-1}-1/4)-t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})\leq\frac{(2s-4)c_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{c_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}=\frac{(2s-3)c_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

(183)

We have thus established (
156a
) and (
156b
).

Finally, we turn to the proof of (
156c
).
In view of (
156b
), one has

\displaystyle t_{\overline{s-2}}(\gamma^{2s-3}-1/4)-t_{\overline{s-2}}(\tau_{s-1})\leq\frac{2sc_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

In addition,

\displaystyle t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})-t_{\overline{s-2}}(\tau_{s-1})

\displaystyle\geq t_{\overline{s-1}}(\gamma\tau_{s-1})-t_{\overline{s-2}}(\tau_{s-1})=t_{s-1}(\tau_{s-1})-t_{\overline{s-2}}(\tau_{s-1})

\displaystyle\geq t_{s-1}({\tau}_{s-1})-t_{\overline{s-2}}(\tau_{s-1})>\frac{2sc_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}},

where the identity in the first line comes from Part (iii) of Lemma
8
,
and the last inequality uses the assumption
t_{s-1}({\tau}_{s-1})>t_{\overline{s-2}}(\tau_{s-1})+\frac{2sc_{0}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}
.
Combining the above two inequalities justifies the validity of the advertised inequality (
156c
).
Then, we establish (
156c
) for
s=3
through Lemma
4
, which gives

\displaystyle t_{\overline{1}}\big(\gamma^{3}-1/4\big)\leq t_{2}(\tau_{2})\leq t_{\overline{2}}(\tau_{3}),

(184)

where the last inequality comes from (
176b
).

Proof of Lemma
19
.

To begin with, the claim (
176a
) holds when
s=2
as a result of the inequality (
38b
) in Lemma
4
.
We now turn to the case with
3\leq s\leq H
.
In view of the property (
59
) in Lemma
8
, we have

\displaystyle\max\Big\{Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0}),Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\Big\}\leq\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\tau_{s}<\tau_{s}\qquad\text{and}\qquad Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})=\gamma V^{(t)}(\overline{s-1}).

Recognizing that
V^{(t)}(s)
is a convex combination of
\big\{Q^{(t)}(s,a)\big\}_{a\in\{a_{0},a_{1},a_{2}\}}
,
we know that if
V^{(t)}(s)\geq{\tau}_{s}
, then one necessarily has
Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})>{\tau}_{s}
, or equivalently,

V^{(t)}(\overline{s-1})>{\tau}_{s}/\gamma\geq\tau_{s}
.
This essentially means that
t_{s}({\tau}_{s})\geq t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})
, thus establishing the claim (
176a
).

Similarly, Lemma
8
(cf. (
61
)) also tells us that

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{0})

\displaystyle=\gamma\tau_{s}\qquad\text{and}\qquad Q^{(t)}(\overline{s},a_{1})=\gamma V^{(t)}(s).

This means that if
V^{(t)}(s-1)\leq{\tau}_{s-1}
,
then

V^{(t)}(\overline{s-1})\leq\max\Big\{Q^{(t)}(\overline{s-1},a_{0}),Q^{(t)}(\overline{s-1},a_{1})\Big\}\leq\gamma{\tau}_{s-1}\leq\tau_{s}.

Consequently, we conclude that
t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})\geq t_{s-1}({\tau}_{s-1})
, as claimed in (
176b
).
∎

Appendix F
Analysis for the blowing-up lemma (Lemma
7
)

In this section, we establish the blowing-up phenomenon as asserted in Lemma
7
.

F.1
Which reference point
t_{\mathsf{ref}}
shall we choose?

Let us specify the time instance
t_{\mathsf{ref}}
as required in Lemma
7
as follows

\displaystyle t_{\mathsf{ref}}\coloneqq\min~\Big\{t\in\big[\,t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}),t_{s}(\tau_{s})\,\big)~\,|\,~c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\leq\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\Big\},

(185)

where
c_{\mathsf{ref}}\in(0,1/3)
is some constant to be specified shortly.

Existence.

An important step is to justify that (
185
) is well-defined, namely, there exists at least one time instance within

\big[\,t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}),t_{s}(\tau_{s})\,\big)
that satisfies
c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\leq\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)
.
Towards this, we note that if the time instance
t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})
obeys

\displaystyle c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\leq\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\qquad\text{when }t=t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}),

then we simply have
t_{\mathsf{ref}}=t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})
. We then move on to the complement case where

\displaystyle c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(a_{0}\,|\,s)

\displaystyle>\pi^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(a_{1}\,|\,s),\quad

\displaystyle\text{or equivalently,}\qquad\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{0})

\displaystyle>\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})-\log(c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)).

(186)

To justify that the construction (
185
) makes sense,
it suffices to show that the endpoint
t_{s}({\tau}_{s})
obeys

\displaystyle c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t_{s}({\tau}_{s}))}(a_{0}\,|\,s)<\pi^{(t_{s}({\tau}_{s}))}(a_{1}\,|\,s).

(187)

In order to validate (
187
), recall that the inequality (
60
) in Lemma
8
ensures that

\pi^{(t_{s}({\tau}_{s}))}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\geq\frac{1-\gamma}{2},

given that
V^{(t_{s}({\tau}_{s}))}(s)\geq{\tau}_{s}
.
Therefore, the inequality (
187
) must be satisfied when
c_{\mathsf{ref}}<1/2
,
given that the left-hand side of (
187
) obeys

c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t_{s}({\tau}_{s}))}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\leq c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)<\frac{1-\gamma}{2}.

This in turn validates the existence of (
187
) for this case.

Several immediate properties about
t_{\mathsf{ref}}
and
t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})
.

We pause to single out a couple of immediate properties about the
t_{\mathsf{ref}}
constructed above as well as
t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})
.

Consider the case where
t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})
obeys

\displaystyle c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(a_{0}\,|\,s)

\displaystyle\leq\pi^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(a_{1}\,|\,s),

\displaystyle\text{or equivalently,}\qquad\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})-\log\big(c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\big),

then one has
t_{\mathsf{ref}}=t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})
(as discussed above).
As can be clearly seen,
t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})
satisfies the advertised inequality (
45a
) by taking
c_{\mathsf{ref}}\geq c_{\mathrm{p}}/8064
.
Additionally, let us first recall from (
156c
) in Lemma
18
that

t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})=\max\Big\{t_{\overline{s-2}}(\gamma^{2s-3}-1/4),~t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})\Big\}.

This combined with Lemma
6
(see (
43
)) tells us that

\displaystyle\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})=\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})\leq\theta^{(t_{s-2}({\tau}_{s-2}))}(s,a_{1})\leq-\frac{1}{2}\log\Big(1+\frac{c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma}{35}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}t_{s-2}({\tau}_{s-2})\Big),

(188)

where the last relation utilizes the bound (
40
) in Lemma
5
.
This leads to the advertised bound (
45b
).

As a result, the claims (
45a
)-(
45b
) only need to be justified under the assumption (
186
).

Organization of the proof.

In light of the above basic facts, the subsequent proof focuses on the scenario where (
186
) is satisfied, namely, the case where

t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})<t_{\mathsf{ref}}.

We shall start by justifying that
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})
has not increased much during
[t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}),t_{\mathsf{ref}}]
, as detailed in Appendix
F.2
and
F.3
(focusing on two separate stages respectively).
This feature will then be used in Appendix
F.4
to establish the claims (
45a
)-(
45b
),
and in Appendix
F.5
to establish the claim (
45c
).

F.2
Stage I: the duration where
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})<\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0})

Suppose that at the starting point we have
\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{2})<\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{0})
; otherwise the reader can proceed directly to Stage II in Appendix
F.3
.
The goal is to control the number of iterations taken to achieve
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\geq\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0})
.
More specifically, let us define the transition point

\displaystyle t_{\mathsf{tran}}\coloneqq\min~\Big\{t\,|\,\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\geq\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0}),\,t\geq t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})\Big\}.

(189)

In this subsection, we seek to develop an upper bound on
t_{\mathsf{tran}}-t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})
, and to show that
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})\leq 1/2
holds throughout this stage.

Preparation: basic facts and rescaled policies.

Before moving forward, we first gather some basic facts.
To begin with, from the definition (
185
) of
t_{\mathsf{ref}}
,
we know that the inequality
c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)>\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)
holds true for every
t\in[t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}),~t_{\mathsf{ref}})
, or equivalently,

\displaystyle\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0})>\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\log\big(c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\big)\qquad\text{for all }t\in[t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}),~t_{\mathsf{ref}}).

(190)

In the case considered here, we have — according to (
190
) and (
189
) — that

\displaystyle\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0})>\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\log\big(c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\big)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0})>\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})

(191)

for any
t
obeying
t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})\leq t<\min\{t_{\mathsf{tran}},t_{\mathsf{ref}}\}
.
This means that

\displaystyle\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0})=\max_{a}\theta^{(t)}(s,a)\qquad\text{and hence}\qquad{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)>1/3

(192)

holds for any
t
obeying
t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})\leq t<\min\{t_{\mathsf{tran}},t_{\mathsf{ref}}\}
, provided that
0<c_{\mathsf{ref}}<1
.

Moreover, let us introduce the rescaled policy
\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a\,|\,s)
as before

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a\,|\,s)\coloneqq\exp\Big(\theta^{(t)}(s,a)-\max_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}\theta^{(t)}(s,a^{\prime})\Big).

In view of (
192
), the rescaled policy can therefore be written as

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{cc}&\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)=\exp\big(\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0})\big)=\exp\big(2\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})+\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\big)\\
&\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)=\exp\big(\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0})\big)=\exp\big(2\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})+\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\big)\end{array}

for any
t
with
t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})\leq t<\min\{t_{\mathsf{tran}},t_{\mathsf{ref}}\}
, where we have used the constraint
\sum_{a}\theta^{(t)}(s,a)=0
(see Part (vii) of Lemma
8
).

Showing
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})\leq 1/2
by induction.

In the following, we seek to prove by induction the following key property

\displaystyle\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})\leq 1/2

(195)

for any
t
that obeys
t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})\leq t\leq\min\{t_{\mathsf{tran}},t_{\mathsf{ref}}\}
and

\displaystyle t-t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})\leq\frac{225}{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}\exp\big(\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})\big)}\eqqcolon\widetilde{t}.

(196)

We shall return to justify (
196
) for all
t
within this stage later on.
In words, the claim (
195
) essentially means that
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})
does not deviate much from
\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})
during this stage.
With regards to the base case where
t=t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})
, the hypothesis (
195
) holds true trivially.
Next, assuming that (
195
) is satisfied for every integer less than or equal to
t-1
,
we intend to establish this hypothesis for the
t
-th iteration, which is accomplished as follows.

First, Lemma
1
and Lemma
10
tell us that
Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-V^{(t)}(s)\leq 1
.
It then follows that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d^{(t)}_{\mu}(s)\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\Big\{Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-V^{(t)}(s)\Big\}\leq 14c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s),

which relies on the bound
d_{\mu}^{(t)}(s)\leq 14c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)^{2}
stated in Lemma
3
.
As a result, it can be derived from the PG update rule (
12a
) that

\displaystyle\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})}^{t-1}\eta\frac{\partial{V}^{(j)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\leq\sum_{j=t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})}^{t-1}14c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta(1-\gamma)\pi^{(j)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)

\displaystyle\leq 14c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta(1-\gamma)(t-t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))\max_{t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})\leq j<t}\pi^{(j)}(a_{1}\,|\,s).

(197)

Regarding the term involving
\pi^{(j)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)
, we observe that for any
t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})\leq j<t
,

\displaystyle\pi^{(j)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\leq}\widehat{\pi}^{(j)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{\leq}\exp\Big(\frac{3}{2}\theta^{(j)}(s,a_{1})\Big)

(198)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(iii)}}{\leq}\exp\Big(\frac{3}{2}\big(\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})+1/2\big)\Big).

(199)

Here, (i) is a consequence of (
132
),
(ii) holds since (in view of (
F.2
),
\theta^{(j)}(s,a_{0})\geq 0
, and
\sum_{a}\theta^{(j)}(s,a)=0
)

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(j)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)

\displaystyle=\exp\Big(2\theta^{(j)}(s,a_{1})+\theta^{(j)}(s,a_{2})\Big)\leq\exp\Big(2\theta^{(j)}(s,a_{1})+0.5\theta^{(j)}(s,a_{2})+0.5\theta^{(j)}(s,a_{0})\Big)

\displaystyle=\exp\Big(1.5\theta^{(j)}(s,a_{1})\Big),

whereas (iii) follows from the induction hypothesis (
195
) for any
t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})\leq j<t
.
Combine the inequalities (
F.2
) and (
199
) to reach

\displaystyle\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})\leq 14c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta(1-\gamma)\big(t-t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})\big)\exp\Big(\frac{3}{2}\big(\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})+1/2\big)\Big).

Consequently, under the constraint (
196
),
the preceding inequality implies that

\displaystyle\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})

\displaystyle\leq~14c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta(1-\gamma)\frac{225}{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}\exp(\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1}))}\exp\Big(\frac{3}{2}\big(\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})+1/2\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=~\frac{3150e\exp\big(\frac{1}{2}\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})\big)}{c_{\mathrm{p}}(1-\gamma)}\leq\frac{1}{2},

(200)

where the last inequality makes use of (
188
) and
the assumption (
44
).
These allow us to establish the induction hypothesis for the
t
-th iteration, namely,

\displaystyle\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})\leq 1/2.

(201)

Validating the constraint (
196
) and upper bounding
\min\{t_{\mathsf{tran}},t_{\mathsf{ref}}\}-t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})
.

It remains to justify the assumed condition (
196
) for all iteration within this stage.
To this end, suppose instead that

\displaystyle t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})+\widetilde{t}\leq\min\{t_{\mathsf{tran}},t_{\mathsf{ref}}\},

(202)

where
\widetilde{t}
is defined in (
196
).
We claim that the following relation is satisfied

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)-\widehat{\pi}^{(t-1)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\geq

\displaystyle~\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{150}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}\Big[\widehat{\pi}^{(t-1)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\Big]^{2}

(203)

for any
t
obeying
t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})\leq t\leq t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})+\widetilde{t}\leq\min\{t_{\mathsf{tran}},t_{\mathsf{ref}}\}
.
Equipped with this recursive relation,
we can invoke Lemma
11
to develop a lower bound on
\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)
, provided that an initial lower bound is available.
In order to do so, in view of the expression (
F.2
), we can deduce that

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(a_{2}\,|\,s)=\exp\Big(2\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{2})+\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})\Big)\geq\exp\Big(\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})\Big),

where the last relation is due to the bound
\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{2})\geq 0
(see (
43
) in Lemma
6
).
Combining the above two inequalities and applying Lemma
11
(see (
69b
)), we arrive at

\pi^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\geq 1/2
— and hence
\pi^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\geq\pi^{(t)}(s,a_{0})
— as soon as
t-t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})
exceeds

\displaystyle\frac{1+\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{100}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}{\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{150}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}\widehat{\pi}^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(a_{2}\,|\,s)}.

This together with the definition of
t_{\mathsf{tran}}
thus indicates that

\displaystyle t_{\mathsf{tran}}-t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1+\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{150}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}{\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{150}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}\widehat{\pi}^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(a_{2}\,|\,s)}\leq\frac{1+\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{150}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}}{\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{150}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}\exp\big(\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})\big)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1.5}{\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{150}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}\exp\big(\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})\big)},

provided that
\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{150}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}\leq 0.5
.
This, however, contradicts the assumption (
202
).
As a consequence, we conclude that
t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})+\widetilde{t}>\min\{t_{\mathsf{tran}},t_{\mathsf{ref}}\}
, thus indicating that

\displaystyle\min\{t_{\mathsf{tran}},t_{\mathsf{ref}}\}-t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})\leq\widetilde{t}\leq\frac{225}{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}\exp\big(\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})\big)}.

(204)

Showing that
t_{\mathsf{tran}}=\min\{t_{\mathsf{tran}},t_{\mathsf{ref}}\}
.

We now justify that
t_{\mathsf{tran}}<t_{\mathsf{ref}}
, so that the upper bound (
204
) leads to an upper bound on
t_{\mathsf{tran}}-t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})
.
Suppose instead that

t_{\mathsf{tran}}\geq t_{\mathsf{ref}},\qquad\text{or equivalently,}\qquad t_{\mathsf{ref}}=\min\{t_{\mathsf{tran}},t_{\mathsf{ref}}\},

and we would like to show that this leads to contradiction.
By definition of
t_{\mathsf{ref}}
, we have

\theta^{(t_{\mathsf{ref}})}(s,a_{0})\leq\theta^{(t_{\mathsf{ref}})}(s,a_{1})-\log\big(c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\big).

This further yields

\displaystyle\max\Big\{\theta^{(t_{\mathsf{ref}})}(s,a_{0}),\theta^{(t_{\mathsf{ref}})}(s,a_{1})\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\theta^{(t_{\mathsf{ref}})}(s,a_{1})-\log\big(c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\big)

\displaystyle\leq\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})+1/2-\log\big(c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\big)<0,

where the second inequality arises from (
195
),
and the last one makes use of (
188
) as long as
t_{s-2}({\tau}_{s-2})
.
However, this together with the constraint
\sum_{a}\theta^{(t_{\mathsf{ref}})}(s,a)=0
implies that

\theta^{(t_{\mathsf{ref}})}(s,a_{2})=-\theta^{(t_{\mathsf{ref}})}(s,a_{0})-\theta^{(t_{\mathsf{ref}})}(s,a_{1})>0>\max\Big\{\theta^{(t_{\mathsf{ref}})}(s,a_{0}),\theta^{(t_{\mathsf{ref}})}(s,a_{1})\Big\}.

which, however, implies that
t_{\mathsf{ref}}>t_{\mathsf{tran}}
(according to the definition of
t_{\mathsf{tran}}
) and leads to contradiction.
As a result, we conclude that

\displaystyle t_{\mathsf{tran}}<t_{\mathsf{ref}},

(205)

and the bound (
204
) then indicates that

\displaystyle t_{\mathsf{tran}}-t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})\leq\frac{225}{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}\exp\big(\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})\big)}.

(206)

F.2.1
Proof of the inequality (
203
)

From the relation (
F.2
), one can deduce that

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)-\widehat{\pi}^{(t-1)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)=

\displaystyle~\exp\Big(2\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})+\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\Big)-\exp\Big(2\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{2})+\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\widehat{\pi}^{(t-1)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\Big\{\exp\Big(2\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-2\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{2})+\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})\Big)-1\Big\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\widehat{\pi}^{(t-1)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\Big\{2\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-2\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{2})+\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\widehat{\pi}^{(t-1)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\cdot\eta\Big(2\frac{\partial{V}^{(t-1)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{2})}+\frac{\partial{V}^{(t-1)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\Big)

(207)

for any
t
with
t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})\leq t\leq\min\{t_{\mathsf{tran}},t_{\mathsf{ref}}\}
, where the inequality above follows from the elementary fact
e^{x}-1\geq x
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
.
Therefore, the difference between
\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)
and
\widehat{\pi}^{(t-1)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)

depends on both
\frac{\partial{V}^{(t-1)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{2})}

and
\frac{\partial{V}^{(t-1)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}
,
motivating us to lower bound these two derivatives separately.

Step 1: bounding
\frac{\partial{V}^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{2})}
.

First, we make the observation that for any
3\leq s<H
and any
t\geq t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})
,

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})=\gamma p\big(V^{(t)}(\overline{s-2})-\gamma\tau_{s-2}\big)\geq\gamma p\big(\gamma^{2s-3}-1/4-\gamma\tau_{s-2}\big)\geq\frac{p}{8}=\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}(1-\gamma)}{8}

(208)

holds as long as
\gamma(\gamma^{2s-3}-1/4-\gamma\tau_{s})\geq 1/8.

Here, the first identity comes from (
59
) in Lemma
8
,
and the first inequality holds for any
t\geq t_{\overline{s-2}}(\gamma^{2s-3}-1/4)
— a consequence of the monotonicity property in Lemma
9
.
As a result, for any
t
obeying
t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})\leq t\leq\min\{t_{\mathsf{tran}},t_{\mathsf{ref}}\}
we have

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-V^{(t)}(s)

\displaystyle=\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})\Big)+\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}(1-\gamma)}{24}-\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\geq\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}(1-\gamma)}{24}-c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\geq\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}(1-\gamma)}{48},

(209)

where the first inequality combines (
208
) with the facts that
\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\geq 1/3
(see (
192
)) and
0\leq Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2}),Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\leq 1
(see Lemma
1
), and the last line holds by observing (see (
185
))

\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\leq c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\leq c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\qquad\text{for all }t\in[t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}),t_{\mathsf{ref}})

and using the assumption
c_{\mathsf{ref}}\leq c_{\mathrm{p}}/2
.
Consequently, for any
t\geq t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})
, the gradient w.r.t.
\theta(s,a_{2})
satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(s)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{2})}=

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d^{(t)}_{\mu}(s)\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})-V^{(t)}(s)\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma}{48}(1-\gamma)^{2}{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\geq\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma}{144}(1-\gamma)^{2}\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)>0,

(210)

where the first inequality above also makes use of the lower bound in Lemma
2
.

In fact, the above lower bound holds true regardless of
t
, as long as
t\geq t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})
where we
have shown that
\frac{\partial V^{(t-1)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{2})}
is bounded from below by
0
.
One can thus conclude that the iterate
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})
increases with
t
.

Step 2: bounding
\frac{\partial{V}^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}
.

Regarding the gradient w.r.t.
\theta(s,a_{1})
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d^{(t)}_{\mu}(s)\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-V^{(t)}(s)\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.90755pt=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d^{(t)}_{\mu}(s)\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\Big\{\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})\Big)+\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\Big)\Big\}

\displaystyle\hskip-28.90755pt\geq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d^{(t)}_{\mu}(s)\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\Big(\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)+\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\Big)(\gamma\tau_{s}-\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\tau_{s}),

where the last line follows since (see Lemma
8
and the fact that
t\geq t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})
)

\displaystyle\max\big\{Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0}),Q^{\pi}(s,a_{2})\big\}\leq\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\tau_{s},\qquad Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})=\gamma V^{(t)}(\overline{s-1})\geq\gamma\tau_{s}.

In addition, recognizing that
\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)+\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\leq 1

and
d_{\mu}^{(t)}(s)\leq 14c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)^{2}
(see Lemma
3
),
we can continue the above bound to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\geq

\displaystyle~-14c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\tau_{s}\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1-\gamma}{1+\sqrt{\gamma}}\geq-7c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)^{2}\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s),

(211)

where the last inequality is due to
\tau_{s}\leq 1/2
and
0<\gamma<1
and the bound (
132
).

Step 3: connecting
\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)
with
\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)
.

The above lower bound (
211
) on
\frac{\partial{V}^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}
is dependent on

\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)
. However, the desired lower bound (
203
) is only a function of
\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)
.
This motivates us to investigate the connection between
\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)
and
\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)
.

To this end, let us write

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(t-1)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)=\widehat{\pi}^{(t-1)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\exp\Big(\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{2})\Big).

(212)

As a result, one only needs to control the quantity
\exp\big(\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{2})\big)
.
In order to do so, we make use of the induction hypothesis (
195
) for the
(t-1)
-th iteration to show that

\displaystyle\exp\Big(\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{2})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big(\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})+1/2-\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{2})\Big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{(i)}}}{{\leq}}\exp\Big(\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{1})+1/2-\theta^{(t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s}))}(s,a_{2})\Big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{(ii)}}}{{\leq}}\exp\Big(\theta^{(t_{s-2}({\tau}_{s-2}))}(s,a_{1})+1/2\Big).

Here, (i) follows from the fact that
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})
increases with
t
(see (
210
));
and (ii) comes from the inequality (
43
) in Lemma
6
as well as (
188
).
Recalling Lemma
5
, one has

\displaystyle\exp\Big(\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t-1)}(s,a_{2})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big(\theta^{(t_{s-2}({\tau}_{s-2}))}(s,a_{1})+1/2\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\exp(1/2)}{\sqrt{1+\frac{c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma}{35}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}t_{s-2}({\tau}_{s-2})}}\leq\frac{\gamma c_{\mathrm{p}}}{1050},

(213)

where the last inequality is satisfied provided that

t_{s-2}({\tau}_{s-2})\geq\frac{1050^{2}e}{\frac{c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma^{3}}{35}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}c_{\mathrm{p}}^{2}}
.
Combining (
211
) with (
212
) and (
F.2.1
),
we arrive at

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{V}^{(t-1)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\geq

\displaystyle~-\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{150}(1-\gamma)^{2}\widehat{\pi}^{(t-1)}(a_{2}\,|\,s).

(214)

Step 4: combining bounds.

Putting the above pieces together and invoking the expression (
F.2.1
) yield for
\gamma>0.96
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)-\widehat{\pi}^{(t-1)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)

\displaystyle\geq\widehat{\pi}^{(t-1)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\cdot\eta\Big(2\frac{\partial{V}^{(t-1)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{2})}+\frac{\partial{V}^{(t-1)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\Big[\widehat{\pi}^{(t-1)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\Big]^{2}\eta\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{150}(1-\gamma)^{2},

which concludes the proof of the advertised bound (
203
).

F.3
Stage II: the duration where
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\geq\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0})

We now turn attention to the case where
t
lies within
[t_{\mathsf{tran}},t_{\mathsf{ref}})
, which is a non-empty interval according to (
205
).
In this case one has

\displaystyle\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\geq\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0}),\qquad\text{or equivalently,}\qquad\pi^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\geq\pi^{(t)}(s,a_{0}),

(215)

as a consequence of the definition (
189
) of
t_{\mathsf{tran}}
.
Again, from the definition (
185
) of
t_{\mathsf{ref}}
,
the inequality
c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)>\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)
holds true
for every
t\in[t_{\mathsf{tran}},~t_{\mathsf{ref}})
, or equivalently,

\displaystyle\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0})\geq\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\log\big(c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\big)\qquad\text{for all }t\in[t_{\mathsf{tran}},~t_{\mathsf{ref}}).

(216)

The goal of this subsection is to show that

\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t_{\mathsf{tran}})}(s,a_{1})\leq 1/2
throughout this stage.

Preparation.

From the above conditions (
215
) and (
216
), we have

\displaystyle\pi^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\geq\pi^{(t)}(s,a_{0})\geq\pi^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\qquad\text{and hence}\qquad\pi^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\geq 1/3.

(217)

We now look at the gradient w.r.t.
\theta(s,a_{0})
, for which we first observe that

\displaystyle Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})-V^{(t)}(s)=

\displaystyle~\pi^{(t)}(a_{2}\,|\,s)\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{2})\Big)+\pi^{(t)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})-Q^{(t)}(s,a_{1})\Big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{(i)}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle-\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}(1-\gamma)}{24}+c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{(ii)}}}{{\leq}}-\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}(1-\gamma)}{36}<0.

(218)

Here, (i) follows from the inequalities (
208
) and (
217
), whereas (ii) holds true as long as
c_{\mathsf{ref}}\leq{c_{\mathrm{p}}}/{72}
. Consequently,

\frac{\partial{V}^{(t)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{0})}=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d^{(t)}_{\mu}(s)\pi^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\Big(Q^{(t)}(s,a_{0})-V^{(t)}(s)\Big)<0,

thus indicating that
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{0})
is decreasing with
t
.

Key induction hypotheses.

Again, we seek to prove by induction that

\displaystyle\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t_{\mathsf{tran}})}(s,a_{1})\leq 1/2,\qquad t\in[t_{\mathsf{tran}},t_{\mathsf{ref}}).

(219)

For the base case where
t=t_{\mathsf{tran}}
, this claim trivially holds true.
Now suppose that the induction hypothesis (
219
) is satisfied for every iteration up to
t-1
,
and we would like to establish it for the
t
-th iteration.
Towards this, we find it helpful to introduce another auxiliary induction hypothesis

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(i)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\leq\frac{1}{1+\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{288}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}(i-t_{\mathsf{tran}})}\qquad\text{for all }i\in[t_{\mathsf{tran}},t).

(220)

As an immediate remark, this hypothesis trivially holds true when
t=t_{\mathsf{tran}}+1
. In what follows, we shall first establish (
219
) for the
t
-th iteration assuming satisfaction of (
220
), and then use to demonstrate that (
220
) holds for
i=t
as well.

Inductive step 1: showing that
\theta^{(t)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t_{\mathsf{tran}})}(s,a_{1})\leq 1/2
.

Towards this, let us introduce for convenience another time instance

\displaystyle\widetilde{t}\coloneqq\arg\max_{i:\,t_{\mathsf{tran}}\leq i<t}\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{1}),

(221)

which reflects the time when
\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{1})
reaches its maximum before iteration
t
.
In order to establish the induction hypothesis (
219
) for the
t
-th iteration,
it is sufficient to demonstrate that

\displaystyle\theta^{(\widetilde{t})}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t_{\mathsf{tran}})}(s,a_{1})\leq 1/2.

(222)

As before, let us employ the PG update rule (
12a
) to expand
\theta^{(\widetilde{t})}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t_{\mathsf{tran}})}(s,a_{1})
as follows

\displaystyle\theta^{(\widetilde{t})}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t_{\mathsf{tran}})}(s,a_{1})=

\displaystyle~\sum_{i=t_{\mathsf{tran}}}^{\widetilde{t}-1}\eta\frac{\partial{V}^{(i)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}.

(223)

For each gradient
\frac{\partial{V}^{(i)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}
, invoking Lemma
3
, Lemma
1
and Lemma
10
tells us that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{V}^{(i)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}=

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{1-\gamma}d^{(i)}_{\mu}(s)\pi^{(i)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\big({Q}^{(i)}(s,a_{1})-{V}^{(i)}(s)\big)\leq 14c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)\pi^{(i)}(a_{1}\,|\,s).

(224)

In addition, a little algebra together with (
217
) leads to

\displaystyle\pi^{(i)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{\pi}^{(i)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)=\exp\Big(\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{2})\Big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{(i)}}}{{=}}\exp\Big(\frac{3}{2}\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{1})+\frac{1}{2}\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{0})-\frac{1}{2}\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{2})\Big)

\displaystyle=\exp\Big(\frac{3}{2}\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{1})\Big)\sqrt{\widehat{\pi}^{(i)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{(ii)}}}{{\leq}}\exp\Big(\frac{3}{2}\theta^{(\widetilde{t})}(s,a_{1})\Big)\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{288}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}(i-t_{\mathsf{tran}})}}

for any
i
obeying
t_{\overline{s-1}}(\tau_{s})\leq i<\widetilde{t}
,
where the first inequality comes from (
132
), (i) makes use of
\sum_{a}\theta^{(i)}(s,a)=0
, and (ii) follows from the induction hypothesis (
220
) along
with the definition (
221
) of
\widetilde{t}
.

Putting the above bounds together with (
223
) and (
224
) guarantees that

\displaystyle\theta^{(\widetilde{t})}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t_{\mathsf{tran}})}(s,a_{1})\leq

\displaystyle~\sum_{i=t_{\mathsf{tran}}}^{\widetilde{t}-1}14c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta(1-\gamma)\exp\Big(\frac{3}{2}\theta^{(\widetilde{t})}(s,a_{1})\Big)\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{288}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}(i-t_{\mathsf{tran}})}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\frac{14c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta\exp\big(\frac{3}{2}\theta^{(\widetilde{t})}(s,a_{1})\big)}{\sqrt{\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{288}\eta}}\left\{1+\sum_{i=t_{\mathsf{tran}}+1}^{\widetilde{t}-1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{i-t_{\mathsf{tran}}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\sqrt{\frac{225792c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta(\widetilde{t}-t_{\mathsf{tran}})}{c_{\mathrm{p}}}}\exp\Big(\frac{3}{2}\theta^{(\widetilde{t})}(s,a_{1})\Big).

(225)

Given that
\theta^{(\widetilde{t})}(s,a_{0})\geq\theta^{(\widetilde{t})}(s,a_{1})-\log\big(c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\big)

(see (
216
)) and
\sum_{a}\theta^{(\widetilde{t})}(s,a)=0
, one obtains

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(\widetilde{t})}(a_{0}\,|\,s)

\displaystyle=\exp\Big(\theta^{(\widetilde{t})}(s,a_{0})-\theta^{(\widetilde{t})}(s,a_{2})\Big)=\exp\Big(2\theta^{(\widetilde{t})}(s,a_{0})+\theta^{(\widetilde{t})}(s,a_{1})\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\exp\Big(3\theta^{(\widetilde{t})}(s,a_{1})-2\log\big(c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\big)\Big),

which combined with the inequality (
220
) thus implies that

\displaystyle\exp\Big(\frac{3}{2}\theta^{(\widetilde{t})}(s,a_{1})\Big)\leq c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\sqrt{\widehat{\pi}^{(\widetilde{t})}(a_{0}\,|\,s)}\leq\frac{c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)}{\sqrt{\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{288}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}(\widetilde{t}-t_{\mathsf{tran}})}}.

(226)

As a consequence of the inequalities (
225
) and (
226
), we obtain

\displaystyle\theta^{(\widetilde{t})}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(t_{\mathsf{tran}})}(s,a_{1})\leq

\displaystyle~\sqrt{\frac{225792c_{\mathrm{m}}\eta(\widetilde{t}-t_{\mathsf{tran}})}{c_{\mathrm{p}}}}\frac{c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)}{\sqrt{\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{288}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}(\widetilde{t}-t_{\mathsf{tran}})}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\frac{8064c_{\mathsf{ref}}}{c_{\mathrm{p}}}<\frac{1}{2},

(227)

where the last line holds as long as
c_{\mathsf{ref}}<c_{\mathrm{p}}/16128
.
This in turn establishes our induction hypothesis (
222
) — and hence
(
221
) for the
t
-th iteration — assuming satisfaction of the hypothesis (
220
).

Inductive step 2: establishing the upper bound (
220
).

The next step is thus to justify the induction hypothesis (
220
) when
i=t
.
To do so, we first pay attention to the dynamics of
\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{0})
for any
t_{\mathsf{tran}}\leq i\leq t
.
Recognizing that
\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{2})=\max_{a}\theta^{(i)}(s,a)
(see (
217
)) and
\sum_{a}\theta^{(i)}(s,a)=0
, we can express

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(i)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)=\exp\Big(\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{0})-\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{2})\Big)=\exp\Big(2\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{0})+\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{1})\Big).

This allows one to obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(i)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)-\widehat{\pi}^{(i+1)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)=\exp\Big(2\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{0})+\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{1})\Big)-\exp\Big(2\theta^{(i+1)}(s,a_{0})+\theta^{(i+1)}(s,a_{1})\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad=\widehat{\pi}^{(i)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\Big\{1-\exp\Big(2\theta^{(i+1)}(s,a_{0})-2\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{0})+\theta^{(i+1)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{1})\Big)\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad=\widehat{\pi}^{(i)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\Big\{1-\exp\Big(2\eta\frac{\partial{V}^{(i)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{0})}+\eta\frac{\partial{V}^{(i)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\Big)\Big\}.

(228)

With the above observation in mind, we claim for the moment the following recursive relation

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(i)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)-\widehat{\pi}^{(i+1)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\geq

\displaystyle~\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{288}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}\Big[\widehat{\pi}^{(i)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\Big]^{2}

(229)

for any
i
obeying
t_{\mathsf{tran}}\leq i<t
,
whose proof is deferred to the end of this section.
If this claim were true, then (
67b
) in Lemma
11
allows us to conclude the desired bound

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(t)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\leq\frac{1}{1+\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{288}\eta(1-\gamma)^{2}(t-t_{\mathsf{tran}})}.

(230)

Proof of the inequality (
229
).

Combining (
218
) and the lower bound on
d^{(i)}_{\mu}(s)
in Lemma
2
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V^{(i)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{0})}\leq

\displaystyle~c_{\mathrm{m}}\gamma(1-\gamma)\pi^{(i)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\Big(Q^{(i)}(s,a_{0})-V^{(i)}(s)\Big)\leq-\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{108}(1-\gamma)^{2}\widehat{\pi}^{(i)}(a_{0}\,|\,s),

where the last inequality also makes use of (
132
).
In addition, invoking the inequalities (
224
) and (
132
) gives

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{V}^{(i)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\leq

\displaystyle~14c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)\pi^{(i)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\leq 14c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)\widehat{\pi}^{(i)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)

(231)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~14c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)\widehat{\pi}^{(i)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\exp\Big(\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{0})\Big).

Recall that for any
i\in[t_{\mathsf{tran}},~t_{\mathsf{ref}})
, one has
\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{0})\geq\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{1})-\log\big(c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma)\big)
,
or equivalently,

\exp\Big(\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{1})-\theta^{(i)}(s,a_{0})\Big)\leq c_{\mathsf{ref}}(1-\gamma).

It thus follows that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{V}^{(i)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\leq

\displaystyle~14c_{\mathsf{ref}}c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)^{2}\widehat{\pi}^{(i)}(a_{0}\,|\,s).

As a result, the above bounds taken collectively lead to

\displaystyle 2\frac{\partial{V}^{(i)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{0})}+\frac{\partial{V}^{(i)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\leq\Big[-\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{56}(1-\gamma)^{2}+14c_{\mathsf{ref}}c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)^{2}\Big]\widehat{\pi}^{(i)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\leq-\frac{c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{112}(1-\gamma)^{2}\widehat{\pi}^{(i)}(a_{0}\,|\,s),

provided that
c_{\mathsf{ref}}/c_{\mathrm{p}}<1/1568
.
In addition, similar to (
231
), we can easily see that

\displaystyle\eta\Big|\frac{\partial{V}^{(i)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\Big|\leq

\displaystyle~14\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)\pi^{(i)}(a_{1}\,|\,s)\leq 14\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)\leq 1/3,

(232a)

\displaystyle\eta\Big|\frac{\partial{V}^{(i)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{0})}\Big|\leq

\displaystyle~14\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)\pi^{(i)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\leq 14\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)\leq 1/3

(232b)

as long as
\eta c_{\mathrm{m}}(1-\gamma)\leq 1/42
.

Substituting the preceding bounds into (
228
),
we immediately arrive at

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{(i)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)-\widehat{\pi}^{(i+1)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\pi}^{(i)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\left\{1-\exp\Big(2\eta\frac{\partial{V}^{(i)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{0})}+\eta\frac{\partial{V}^{(i)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\Big)\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\widehat{\pi}^{(i)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\frac{\eta}{2}\Big(-2\frac{\partial{V}^{(i)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{0})}-\frac{\partial{V}^{(i)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\eta c_{\mathrm{p}}c_{\mathrm{m}}}{224}(1-\gamma)^{2}\Big[\widehat{\pi}^{(i)}(a_{0}\,|\,s)\Big]^{2},

where the first inequality holds due to the fact
-1\leq 2\eta\frac{\partial{V}^{(i)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{0})}+\eta\frac{\partial{V}^{(i)}(\mu)}{\partial\theta(s,a_{1})}\leq 0

as well as the elementary inequality
1-e^{x}\geq-x/2
as long as
-1\leq x\leq 0
.
